● PHASE 7 — COMPLETE

  Builder

  - Route: /custom, replacing the Phase 5 placeholder. One route, not
    /custom/[step] — a step is transient UI state, and a URL per step invites
    bookmarking a configuration nothing persists.
  - Steps: the five in UX.md §5 — Produit · Emplacement · Design ·
    Personnalisation · Récapitulatif.
  - Product selection: the Phase 6 isCustomizable gate applied in the query;
    DTOs only, no second product query implementation.
  - Options: rendered from the product's own ProductOption/ProductOptionValue
    rows; placements and sizes from the product's CustomizationProfile, so a
    cap's placements differ from a tote's. Nothing hard-coded.
  - Customization: builder state is shaped as the inputs to a 
    CustomizationConfig — every field maps to a column — so submission is a
    projection, not a translation. No second model.
  - Upload UX: UploadDropzone + FileCard. Files enter client state only; PNG/JPG
    preview, SVG/PDF file-card (ADR-011). Nothing is uploaded, stored, or sent
    to any provider — an E2E test asserts every POST stays on our own origin.
  - Quantity: existing QuantitySelector; the discount is computed server-side
    and rendered as its own line.
  - Review: a spec-sheet summary with per-row "Modifier" jumping back with state
    intact.
  - Submission: CustomRequest + CustomRequestItem + CustomizationConfig + thread
    colours + the first CustomRequestEvent, in one transaction.

  Pricing

  - Domain functions used: domain/money.applyBasisPoints,
    domain/discount.selectDiscountTier/quantityDiscount,
    domain/pricing.resolveUnitPrice,
    domain/pricing/customization.{customizationUnitPrice, setupFeeFor, 
    priceCustomLine, estimateFor}, domain/customization/{pricing-mode, 
    validation}. No pricing exists in React.
  - Estimate behavior: shown at every step including quote mode (§2.4.1) — exact
    in FIXED, a ±15% band from estimateBandPercent in QUOTE (§2.4.2),
    snapshotted on the config and request (§2.4.3).
  - Discount behavior: tiers from discount_tiers, applied to the embroidery 
    component only and shown as an explicit line. A quantity with no tier
    returns a typed error, never a silent zero (§2.5.2).
  - Quote-required behavior: all seven conditions of §2.3, returning every
    failing one, surfaced to the customer as "À cause de la quantité demandée…"
    so a quote never reads as a stall.

  Security

  - Server validation: Zod on the payload, then domain/customization/validation,
    then every reference re-read from the database.
  - Client/server boundary: identifiers in, conclusions out. The client sends
    slugs, ids and counts; the server returns the number.
  - Client-supplied pricing protection: price, estimate, discount, deposit and
    pricingMode are absent from the schema, so a payload carrying them fails to
    parse. A test submits pricingMode: 'FIXED' at quantity 11 and asserts the
    stored mode is QUOTE and the stored estimate is the server's.
  - Option/product validation: the product must be published and customisable;
    the variant must belong to it and be active; the placement and size must
    belong to that product's profile; every thread colour is re-read by id.

  Accessibility

  - Keyboard: every control operable; radiogroups for single choice, checkbox
    roles for the palette.
  - Focus: moves to the step heading on every change — Text/Heading gained ref
    forwarding for this.
  - Forms: FormField owns label/description/error wiring; the disabled submit
    carries its reason in text.
  - Upload: dropzone and file cards keep their Phase 4 semantics; rejections are
    announced.
  - Stepper/progress: a nav with an ordered list and aria-current="step"; steps
    ahead are unreachable.
  - axe: zero WCAG 2.1 A/AA violations at all five steps.

  Responsive

  - 375 / 768 / 1024 / 1440: verified. Horizontal overflow: 0 at every step,
    asserted at 375px across the whole flow.

  Architecture

  - Database coupling: Prisma in exactly three files — lib/db/client.ts and the
    two repositories. No component imports the database, auth, a server module
    or domain/.
  - Domain boundary: the service reads nothing itself; the repository owns
    queries, domain/ owns rules.
  - Submission boundary: client state → serialisable payload → Zod → domain → 
    repository → database.

  Tests

  - Unit: 34 new domain tests at exact boundaries (3/4 colours, 10/11 quantity,
    199/200 instructions, 8/9 colour ceiling, 24/25 setup-fee waiver, every tier
    edge).
  - Component: 17 builder-state and file-rule tests, including one asserting the
    client state holds no price, estimate or mode.
  - Database/integration: 19 adversarial submission tests.
  - E2E: 12 builder tests — full walk-through, submission, estimate reacting to
    a threshold, file accepted without upload, keyboard, axe at each step,
    mobile overflow. 58 total.
  - Format / Lint / Typecheck / Build / Architecture boundaries: all pass.

  Documentation

  tasks/CURRENT.md (TASK-011), docs/DATABASE.md §14 (the four new settings keys
  and why they were added), tests/integration/database.test.ts (the
  exhaustive-key assertion).

  Issues / deviations

  1. Four settings keys added. BUSINESS-RULES.md §2.2 reads the thread-colour
     surcharge, text surcharge and setup fee "from settings", but §14 — which
     calls itself exhaustive — never named them. Added, seeded as placeholders 
     pending the owner, documented.
  2. Embroidery reference data seeded with placeholder prices, in the same sense
     as the Phase 2 shipping rates. The builder cannot exist without
     placements, sizes and a palette; the amounts are the owner's call.
  3. nextRequestNumber is not sequence-backed. Two simultaneous submissions
     would collide on the unique index. DATABASE.md §1 calls for a sequence;
     needed before launch.
  4. A request with an attached file is always QUOTE. Condition 7 requires files
     to be READY; with no storage none can be. Honest rather than a fudge — and
     the confirmation says the file will be requested by e-mail.
  5. Deferred deliberately: draft persistence, multi-item requests, placement
     diagrams, the design-on-product preview, and everything downstream of the
     request.
  6. A stale build cost time twice (Phase 6 and here) — pkill does not exist in
     this shell and Playwright's reuseExistingServer served an old bundle.
     Resolved by killing listeners via PowerShell before each full run.

  Phase 8 readiness

  - YES
  - domain/money, domain/discount and domain/pricing are in place and
    boundary-tested, and the submission path establishes the exact client/server
    pattern that checkout needs with more at stake.

● Audit complete. I ran the suites, re-ran the boundary probes, and read the
  implementation against the docs. Nothing was changed — the implementation is
  sound.

  PHASE 7 POST-COMPACTION AUDIT

  Persistence

  - Transaction: one interactive prisma.$transaction in
    customization-repository.ts:525. nextRequestNumber() runs before it (a
    read); every write is inside it.
  - Records created: CustomizationConfig (with its CustomizationThreadColor rows
    created as a nested create, positioned 0..n), then CustomRequest with a
    nested items: [CustomRequestItem] and events: [CustomRequestEvent]
    (STATUS_CHANGE → SUBMITTED, actorType: CUSTOMER, isCustomerVisible: true).
    Five record types, one call.
  - Rollback behavior: correct by construction — the config id is only reachable
    inside the transaction, so a failure on the customRequest.create (including
    a requestNumber unique-index collision) aborts the whole thing and leaves
    no orphan config. Verified by inspection, not by a forced-failure test (see
    Findings → Low).

  Business rules

  - Pricing: domain/pricing/customization.priceCustomLine only. Rates arrive as
    parameters from settings; nothing is hard-coded. §2.2 formula matches line
    for line, setup fee charged once per configuration, discount applied to the
    embroidery component only.
  - Quote decision: domain/customization/pricing-mode.determinePricingMode, all
    seven §2.3 conditions, returning every failing reason. Thresholds hold at
    the exact documented edges (>3 colours, >10 units, ≥200 chars — "shorter
    than", so 200 already quotes).
  - Availability: isCustomizable + status ACTIVE + deletedAt null + publishedAt 
    ≤ now inside the query, in both listCustomizableProducts and
    resolveRequestReferences.
  - Validation: domain/customization/validation.validateConfiguration, returning
    every issue at once. No rule is duplicated in React, in the action, or in
    the repository.

  Security

  Traced the full path: builder.tsx (client) → actions.ts 'use server' →
  custom-request-service (Zod → domain) → customization-repository → Prisma. The
  client sends identifiers only.

  - Product validation: draft/unpublished refused (product query returns nothing
    → PRODUCT); non-customisable refused by the same predicate; a variant from
    another product refused because the variant is looked up within
    product.variants and must be isActive.
  - Option validation: placement and size are re-read through
    profilePlacement/profileEmbroiderySize scoped to that product's
    customizationProfileId, both requiring isActive — a cap's placement cannot
    be requested on a tote. Thread colours are re-read by id with isActive, and
    threadColors.length !== input.threadColorIds.length fails the whole request,
    so a non-existent or inactive colour cannot slip through. Quantity is
    bounded twice (Zod 1–1000 integer, then validateConfiguration against
    maxQuantity); instructions bounded twice (Zod ≤2000, then the limit from
    settings).
  - Pricing tampering: structurally impossible. customRequestInput has no price,
    total, estimateMin/Max, discountBasisPoints, depositAmount or pricingMode
    field, so those keys are stripped by the schema rather than ignored by
    convention. The integration test submits all seven at once with quantity: 11
    and asserts the persisted mode is QUOTE and the persisted estimate is the
    server's number.
  - File handling: the payload accepts {name, contentType, sizeBytes} and
    nothing else — no bytes, no base64, no data URL. Nothing calls Cloudinary or
    any storage API (grep for fetch|FormData|upload|cloudinary under custom/
    returns only the UploadDropzone import). No design_files row is written, so
    no fake READY record exists. file-rules.ts documents itself as a
    convenience, not a control, and a component test asserts a spoofed MIME type
    passes the client check on purpose.

  CustomizationConfig

  - Result: conforms. The write targets the documented customization_configs
    columns directly (productId, variantId, placementId, embroiderySizeId,
    embroideryText, threadColorCount, instructions, pricingMode,
    pricingModeReason, estimateMin/Max) plus the customization_thread_colors
    join. Free-form fields (freeformProductType, customPlacementNote,
    embroideryWidthMm/HeightMm) are passed as explicit null into the domain
    inputs rather than modelled a second way, and the client's BuilderState is a
    set of inputs to those same columns. No competing representation. ADR-016
    snapshot columns are correctly left unwritten — freezing happens when a
    quote is sent, which is Phase 10.

  Request numbering

  - Current implementation: still read-then-increment — findFirst on the highest
    DEM-<year>- number, +1, zero-padded. Not sequence-backed. DATABASE.md §1
    requires "sequence-backed" human identifiers.
  - Risk: two concurrent submissions read the same maximum and the second
    violates the requestNumber unique index. The failure mode is a rejected 
    submission inside a transaction that rolls back cleanly — a lost request,
    never a corrupt or duplicated one. At this phase's traffic (one public form,
    no launch) the window is negligible.
  - Required before Phase 8: NO. Phase 8 is checkout and orders; it consumes no
    request numbers and has its own "Order number generation" line item. The
    real risk is pattern contagion — Phase 8 must introduce a proper Postgres
    sequence for orderNumber and retrofit requestNumber onto it, rather than
    copying this.

  Placeholder data

  - Settings: the four Phase 7 keys (threadColorSurchargePerColor 150,
    textSurcharge 250, setupFee 1200, setupFeeWaiverQuantity 25) are documented
    in DATABASE.md §14 with the paragraph explaining why they arrived, each row
    marked placeholder; each seed row carries PLACEHOLDER pending the owner in
    its description, so the marking survives into the admin UI, not just the
    docs. loadBuilderSettings throws when one is missing rather than defaulting
    — a silent fallback would price something the business never agreed to. No
    production claim anywhere.
  - Embroidery reference data: 6 placements, 3 sizes, 14 thread colours, 3
    fonts, one default profile. Deterministic and idempotent — every write is an
    upsert on a natural key (code), and the profile is findFirst-then-create.
    Prices are labelled placeholders in code comments, in the profile's own
    description ("Valeurs de démonstration"), and in the seed's console summary
    ("placeholder prices").

  Tests

  - Actual totals: 266 passed / 266, 12 files (verified this session), and 58 
    Playwright passed (verified this session, exit 0). Breakdown: 34
    unit/customization-domain, 19 integration/custom-request, 17
    components/builder, 12 builder E2E.
  - Critical security coverage: genuinely present, not nominal — draft slug,
    non-customisable product, non-existent product, foreign variant,
    non-placement UUID, non-existent thread colour, 9 colours against a ceiling
    of 8, quantity 0/−5/1.5/100000, 2001-char instructions, invalid and empty
    email, and two client-supplied-money tests (one asserting the stored values,
    one asserting two payloads price identically). "Creates nothing when it
    refuses" asserts the row count is unchanged after refusals.
  - Business-rule boundaries: exact, not approximate — tiers at
    9/10/24/25/49/50, colours at 3/4 and 8/9, quantity at 10/11, instructions at
    199/200, setup-fee waiver at 24/25, and "never quotes a negative floor".
  - E2E: full five-step walk-through, real submission returning a reference,
    estimate reacting to a threshold change, a file accepted into the browser
    with an assertion that every POST stays on the page's own origin, rejected
    file type, quantity stepper, keyboard operation and focus movement.
  - Accessibility: axe at all five steps, zero violations; 375px
    horizontal-overflow assertion at every step.
  - Architecture: re-ran both probes. Domain probe → 8 errors, exit 1. Component
    probe → 5 errors, exit 1. grep over src/components/ for
    @/lib/db|@/lib/auth|better-auth|@/server/|@/domain/|generated/prisma → none.
    Prisma is imported by exactly three non-generated files: lib/db/client.ts
    and the two repositories (lib/auth/* imports the shared prisma instance from
    lib/db, not Prisma itself).

  Findings

  - Critical: none.
  - High: none.
  - Medium:
    a. The Prisma-confinement lint rule does not cover this project's generated 
       client path. The global rule lists @prisma/client and .prisma/*; the
       generated client lives at @/generated/prisma. I proved it: a file at
       src/app/__generated_probe.ts importing @/generated/prisma/client lints
       clean, exit 0. src/components/** is protected (its own list includes the
       path), and no code violates the rule today — but the boundary that
       matters most is unguarded outside components. One pattern added to that
       rule closes it.
    b. The builder renders the default profile's placements for every product.
       page.tsx calls loadBuilderReferenceData(null) once; the client never
       reloads per product, while the server resolves against the selected
       product's profile. Harmless today (the seed attaches one profile to all
       customisable products) and it fails closed. But UX §5's "a cap has no
       back placement" is not actually delivered, and the day a second profile
       exists the UI would offer a placement the server rejects at submit.
    c. createCustomRequest is an unauthenticated public write with no rate 
       limit. Consistent with SECURITY.md, which places all rate limiting in
       Phase 15 — but this is the first spammable write endpoint, and
       quoteEstimate issues ~6 queries per configuration change from an
       anonymous caller.
  - Low:
    a. No test forces a failure between the two creates; rollback is verified by
       construction, and "creates nothing when it refuses" only covers
       pre-write refusal.
    b. custom_requests.userId is always null — a signed-in customer submits as a
       guest. Matches the guest pattern; blocks portal linkage until auth is
       wired in.
    c. tasks/CURRENT.md says the component probe reports 6 errors. It reports 5;
       tests/README.md says 5 and is right.
    d. Omitting variantSku prices from basePrice rather than a variant override
       — but the same omission trips condition 1 and forces QUOTE, and estimates
       are non-binding, so there is nothing to gain.
    e. embroideryFontId is never written though three fonts are seeded; font
       selection is simply not in the builder yet.

  Phase 7 status

  - PASS. No incorrectness and no insecurity found; implementation left
    unchanged.

  Phase 8 readiness

  - YES
  - The submission boundary holds under adversarial testing and establishes
    exactly the identifiers-in/money-out pattern checkout needs — with the one
    carry-over that Phase 8's order numbering must introduce a real database
    sequence rather than repeat nextRequestNumber's read-then-increment.

    0 under src/app.

  - E2E: 58 passed — but not on the first attempt, and the detail matters. The
    builder's axe test failed on step 4 with a contrast violation of 3.5:1 on a
    selected thread-colour swatch. It is not an accessibility defect: the
    settled colours are #fbf9f5 on #12100e, 18.05:1, and the reported pair
    (#b4b2af on #585552) is neither token — it is an interpolation. The scan ran
    immediately after the click that toggles the swatch and sampled the 140 ms
    colour transition mid-flight. Run in isolation it failed 5 times out of 5;
    inside the full suite it passed, because a busier machine let the transition
    finish first — the check was measuring load, not contrast. scan() now waits
    for CSS transitions to settle before analysing; the same isolated repeat
    then passed 5 of 5, and the full suite passed 58 of 58. tasks/CURRENT.md
    records this.
  - Build: pass — compiled, TypeScript clean, /custom still static with 5m
    revalidate.

  # Critical architecture rules                                                 
                                                                                
  The architecture must remain:                                                 
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
  UI                                                                            
   ↓                                                                            
  server action / application service                                           
   ↓                                                                            
  domain                                                                        
   ↓                                                                            
  repository                                                                    
   ↓                                                                            
  Prisma                                                                        
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  Never:                                                                        
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
  component → Prisma                                                            
  component → repository                                                        
  component → business pricing                                                  
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  Prisma must remain confined to the approved persistence layer.                
                                                                                
  The client sends **identifiers and user inputs only**.                        
                                                                                
  The server recalculates:                                                      
                                                                                
  * prices                                                                      
  * discounts                                                                   
  * shipping                                                                    
  * totals                                                                      
  * deposit                                                                     
  * stock availability                                                          
  * order eligibility                                                           
                                                                                
  Never trust client-supplied money or totals.                                  
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  # 1. Cart                                                                     
                                                                                
  Implement the documented V1 cart behavior.                                    
                                                                                
  Support:                                                                      
                                                                                
  ### Stock products                                                            
                                                                                
  A customer can add a valid published product variant to the cart with:        
                                                                                
  * product/variant identifier                                                  
  * quantity                                                                    
                                                                                
  The server must verify:                                                       
                                                                                
  * product exists                                                              
  * product is published/active                                                 
  * variant belongs to product                                                  
  * variant is active                                                           
  * requested quantity is valid                                                 
  * stock is available                                                          
                                                                                
  Do not trus                                                                   
  ──── (338 lines hidden) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  ailed transaction leaves no partial order                                     
  * stock reservation rolls back when order creation fails

  invariants.

● PHASE 8 RESULT

  Cart

  - Stock cart: /cart, backed by cart-repository and cart-service. Adding sends
    a slug, an SKU and a quantity; the server re-reads the product (published,
    active, not deleted), matches the variant within that product, requires it
    active, validates the quantity against domain/cart, and caps it at
    stockQuantity − reservedQuantity. Nothing is reserved on add — §10.3. A
    repeated variant increments rather than duplicates, guaranteed by a partial
    unique index rather than by the code remembering.
  - Quote-bound cart: enforced server-side, not hidden. domain/cart.isMutable
    refuses every mutation on QUOTE_BOUND; pricing uses lockedUnitPrice and
    ignores the catalogue (§2.7); no rate lookup happens (§4.6/5A.3 —
    shippingRateId stays null, the label is "Livraison devisée"); quantity
    discounts are not re-applied (5A.5); coupons are refused by acceptsCoupons
    and by a check constraint. The reconstructed total must equal
    quote.grandTotal exactly or placement aborts (5A.6). Acceptance itself is
    Phase 10, so this path is reachable today only from the integration tests,
    which build the state acceptance will leave.
  - Ownership: the documented model, unchanged — userId xor anonymousToken, now
    enforced by carts_owner_check. The guest token is an HttpOnly cookie. One
    active standard cart per user (partial unique index), any number of
    quote-bound carts beside it. mergeGuestCartIntoUserCart sums quantities
    capped at stock, and a quote-bound cart never merges.
  - Server-authoritative pricing: one path,
    server/services/cart-pricing.priceCart, used by the cart, the checkout
    summary, the delivery quote and placement. price, subtotal, discount,
    shipping, total and deposit are absent from every Zod schema, so a payload
    carrying them fails to parse.

  Checkout

  - Guest checkout: the default and the only path built. No account is offered
    before purchase. Email is required and validated; phone is validated against
    Algerian formats because it is the operational contact.
  - Authenticated checkout: compatible by construction — CartOwner resolves to
    {kind:'user'} when a session exists, the order carries userId, and the
    confirmation authorises by ownership instead of a guest token. Not exercised
    through the UI, because sign-in screens are a later phase.
  - Shipping: domain/shipping over shipping_zones/shipping_rates. freeOverAmount
    is the only threshold source and compares against the subtotal after
    discount (4.2, 4.2.1). An unmapped wilaya blocks with an explicit message
    and never falls back (4.4). Selecting a wilaya re-quotes on the server and
    returns the whole totals for each delivery type, so the browser adds nothing
    up.
  - Payment method: COD, bank transfer and CCP. availablePaymentMethods removes
    COD when the cart has a custom line. No provider, no gateway, no fake
    success — the confirmation states the amount due and that the order number
    is the transfer reference (8.10.1).

  Stock

  - Atomic reservation: one statement — UPDATE product_variants SET 
    reserved_quantity = reserved_quantity + n WHERE id = … AND is_active AND 
    stock_quantity - reserved_quantity >= n. Zero rows matched aborts the
    transaction. There is no read-check-write anywhere. A check constraint
    reserved_quantity BETWEEN 0 AND stock_quantity backs it.
  - Rollback behavior: reservation, cart claim, order, items, address and
    history are one $transaction. A test forces a violated line_total constraint
    after the reservation and asserts: no order, no orphan items, reserved
    quantity unchanged, and the cart still ACTIVE rather than falsely converted.
  - Concurrency tests: two simultaneous checkouts for a single unit — exactly
    one succeeds, the other returns OUT_OF_STOCK, and reserved <= stock holds.
    Six simultaneous placements yield six distinct order numbers. A double
    submission of one cart yields one order.

  Orders

  - Order creation transaction: claim the cart (WHERE status = 'ACTIVE' — this,
    not a prior check, is what makes a double submission impossible) → reserve →
    next_order_number() → create order, items, address, first history row →
    link the cart to the order.
  - Historical snapshots: nameSnapshot, variantLabelSnapshot, skuSnapshot,
    imageKeySnapshot, unitPrice, lineTotal and all order totals, plus
    shippingRateLabel so a delivery charge stays explainable after the rate
    table is edited. A test renames the product and triples its price after
    placement and asserts the order renders byte-identically.
  - Deposit: domain/order/deposit — the quote's value copied when there is one
    (5.5.1), zero with no custom line, otherwise applyBasisPoints(grandTotal, 
    5000), clamped at the total. A custom order asking for depositRequired: 1 is
    stored at 50%.
  - Order number sequence: order_number_seq + next_order_number(),
    CMD-YYYY-NNNN. Not MAX(), not a row count, never generated client-side,
    unique index retained as the last defence. The request-number retrofit 
    shipped in the same migration, with setval past the highest number Phase 7
    had already issued. quoteNumber remains without one — recorded for Phase 10.

  Security

  - Client-money tampering: structurally impossible. A test posts grandTotal: 1,
    shippingTotal: 0, discountTotal: 999999, depositRequired: 0, status: 
    'CONFIRMED', paymentStatus: 'PAID' and orderNumber: 'CMD-0000-0001', and
    asserts every stored value is the server's.
  - Stock race: covered above; the guarantee comes from the WHERE clause, not
    from timing.
  - Duplicate submission: the conditional cart claim. One order, cart CONVERTED,
    second attempt refused.
  - Validation: Zod at the boundary, domain/ for meaning, every reference
    re-read. A cart line id belonging to another owner resolves to nothing
    because the lookup is scoped in the query.

  UI

  - Cart: real empty state with routes out, line editing and removal, per-line
    issues explained, a summary showing the discount as its own line, and a
    disabled checkout button with its reason in text when a line cannot be
    bought.
  - Checkout: one page, three sections and a sticky summary (UX.md §7). Errors
    are attached to their fields. The mixed stock-and-custom warning is stated
    before committing (8.9.3), and the deposit is shown as "due now / due on
    delivery".
  - Confirmation: order number, itemised snapshot summary, delivery address,
    what happens next, and — for a deposit order — the amount due with the order
    number as the payment reference.
  - Responsive: 375 / 768 / 1024 / 1440. Zero horizontal overflow asserted at
    375px across cart → checkout → confirmation.
  - Accessibility: axe clean on the empty cart, the full cart, checkout,
    checkout with delivery options, and the confirmation. Keyboard operation
    tested. Two real fixes came out of this: the delivery and payment
    radiogroups had no accessible name, and "Adresse" was ambiguous next to
    "Adresse e-mail" — now "Adresse de livraison".

  Tests

  Counts are from the final run, not carried over.

  - Unit: 109 (39 new in commerce-domain.test.ts — exact boundaries: the
    free-shipping threshold at 999 999/1 000 000, every transition in the 8.1
    table plus the tempting shortcuts, ON_HOLD releasing only to where it was
    held from, the deposit rounding, the payment-status branch order).
  - Integration: 140 against PostgreSQL (36 new in checkout.test.ts).
  - E2E: 73 passed (15 new in checkout.spec.ts), no flakes across a repeat run.
  - Architecture: domain probe 8 errors, component probe 5, generated-client
    probe 1 in each of app/, domain/, components/ — all exit 1. Prisma is
    imported by exactly five files: lib/db/client.ts and the four repositories.
    No component imports the database, auth, a server module or domain/.
  - Build: passes. /cart, /checkout and /order/[orderNumber] are dynamic; the
    catalogue keeps its ISR.
  - Also: migrations replayed onto a fresh database, seed run twice, and the
    full integration suite green against it.

  Documentation

  - Updated: docs/DECISIONS.md (ADR-022 reservation timing, ADR-023
    sequence-backed identifiers, plus the index), docs/ARCHITECTURE.md (the
    checkout transaction boundary and pricing authority), docs/DATABASE.md
    (identifiers now implemented; the cart and order constraints are real),
    docs/BUSINESS-RULES.md (10.4.1, reconciling 10.4 with 8.10.3),
    tasks/CURRENT.md (TASK-012 and the next-phase pointer). docs/UX.md needed no
    clarification.

  Findings

  - Critical: none.
  - High: none.
  - Medium:
    a. A deposit order's stock is validated but not held between placement and
       admin confirmation (ADR-022). Limited today because blanks default to
       MADE_TO_ORDER, but it is the §10.9 case and needs the admin workspace to
       resolve.
    b. The CONFIRMED transition does not exist yet, so the deferred reservation
       has no trigger. The state machine that will drive it is written and
       tested; the admin half of Phase 8 is not built.
    c. Checkout is unauthenticated and unthrottled, like the Phase 7 request
       endpoint. Consistent with SECURITY.md, which puts all rate limiting in
       Phase 15, but placement now creates orders and reserves stock.
  - Low:
    a. The header cart badge shows no count — a cookie read in the storefront
       layout would opt every page beneath it out of static rendering.
    b. estimatedDeliveryFrom/To are null at placement (4.5 counts them from
       confirmedAt), and the production lead time 4.5 requires has no settings
       key.
    c. A client component importing a 'use server' module pulls the database
       into a jsdom test's graph; storefront.test.tsx now stubs the action.
    d. Two Phase 5 E2E tests asserting "there is no cart" were rewritten, and
       useRouter was dropped from the purchase panel because nothing there
       renders cart state.
    e. Test reliability, found and fixed rather than re-run: the checkout suite
       shared the seeded variant's twelve units, and a crashed run leaked
       fixture products into the catalogue — which broke a Phase 7 builder test.
       The suite now owns its stock and sweeps stale fixtures before and after.

  Phase 8 status

  - PASS

  Phase 9 readiness

  - YES
  - The order pipeline is complete and transactionally verified end to end, and
    Phase 9's file storage attaches to an order that already exists — with the
    one carry-over that deposit proof upload and the admin confirmation it feeds
    still need the admin workspace this phase deliberately left out.

  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  # Goal                                                                        
                                                                                
  Implement a secure production-ready media pipeline for customer-submitted     
  embroidery designs.                                                           
                                                                                
  The intended flow is:                                                         
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
  Customer selects design                                                       
          ↓                                                                     
  Browser uploads file                                                          
          ↓                                                                     
  Server validates metadata/content                                             
          ↓                                                                     
  Storage provider                                                              
          ↓                                                                     
  DesignFile record                                                             
          ↓                                                                     
  CustomRequest / CustomizationConfig reference                                 
          ↓                                                                     
  Admin/downstream workflows can access the file                                
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  The architecture must preserve the existing separation:                       
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
  UI                                                                            
   ↓                                                                            
  server action / application service                                           
   ↓                                                                            
  domain validation                                                             
   ↓                                                                            
  media/storage service                                                         
   ↓                                                                            
  repository                                                                    
   ↓                                                                            
  Prisma                                                                        
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  Do not allow components to access Prisma, storage credentials, or server      
  repositories directly.                                                        
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  # 1. Storage provider                                                         
                                                                                
  First inspect whether the project already contains a storage                  
  abstraction/provider decision.                                                
                                                                                
  If the docs already specify the provider, implement that provider.            
                                                                                
  If the provider is still intentionally unspecified, **do not silently choose  
  a production provider**. Instead:                                             
                                                                                
  * implement the provider-neutral storage interface                            
  * implement the local/test adapter needed for automated tests                 
  * document the production-provider decision as a blocker if necessary         
                                                                                
  If Cloudinary is already the documented V1 provider, use the existing planned 
  Cloudinary integration and keep all provider-specific code behind the         
  media/storage boundary.                                                       
                                                                                
  Never expose:                                                                 
                                                                                
  * API secrets                                                                 
  * storage credentials                                                         
  * signed credentials                                                          
  * private storage keys                                                        
                                                                                
  to the browser.                                                               
                                                                                
  ---

   selected provider.

● PHASE 9 RESULT

  Storage

  - Provider: the documented one — S3-compatible object storage, Cloudflare R2
    (ADR-009, ARCHITECTURE.md §9). Cloudinary appears nowhere in the docs; it
    was only ever named in earlier phase briefs as something not to build.
    Implemented in lib/storage/s3.ts, which is the only file in the project that
    imports a storage SDK.
  - Upload architecture: the documented three-phase flow. Browser asks for
    permission → server validates the intent, generates a key and writes a
    PENDING row → browser PUTs directly to storage via a presigned URL bound to
    one key, one content type and one maximum length → browser reports
    completion → the server reads the stored bytes and decides. The application
    never handles file bytes on the request path.
  - Local/test strategy: a filesystem adapter with the same contract, signing
    URLs with an HMAC over key/method/type/length/expiry, plus
    /api/storage/local playing the provider's part. It is not a mock — the
    integration tests drive the real three-step sequence, and an oversized or
    retyped body is refused there exactly as R2 would refuse it. The route
    disappears the moment a bucket is configured.

  File security

  - Server validation: filename sanitised (path segments, control characters,
    length) and never used as a key; extension, declared MIME and magic number
    must all agree; size re-verified by a HEAD after upload; per-file,
    per-configuration and per-owner ceilings.
  - Magic-byte validation: PNG, JPEG and PDF by signature; SVG by requiring the
    leading text to be an XML document whose root is <svg>, tolerating a BOM,
    declaration and comments but nothing else. A magic number appearing later in
    the file does not count.
  - Size limits: 25 MB per file, 5 per configuration, 40 MP / 12 000 px per edge
    on decode. The client's 10 MB limit contradicted the documented 25 MB, so
    file-rules.ts now re-exports the domain constants rather than restating them
    — a limit that exists twice will eventually disagree with itself.
  - SVG/PDF handling: an SVG carrying a script, an on* handler, a foreignObject,
    a DOCTYPE/entity, a javascript: URL, an @import or an external reference is
    refused with a reason, its object deleted. What passes is stored
    byte-for-byte. PDFs are opaque — only the page count is read, from
    structural markers, with no PDF parser on the server. Neither is ever
    rasterised, rendered, or served as anything but application/octet-stream
    with attachment and nosniff.

  Ownership

  - Guest: ownerTokenHash, the SHA-256 of the same anonymous token that 
    identifies a guest cart. One browser is one guest across uploads, cart and
    orders — not a second identity system. Hashed, unlike carts.anonymousToken,
    so a database dump is not a set of usable bearer credentials.
  - Authenticated: ownerUserId, from the session. A new migration adds the
    column, a check that exactly one owner is set on a live row, and the
    indexes.
  - Cross-request protection: every read is scoped in the query. A foreign id
    returns "not found", indistinguishable from an id that never existed, so the
    endpoint is not an enumeration oracle. A client never receives a storage
    key — only an opaque DesignFile.id.

  Lifecycle

  - States: the existing enum, unchanged — PENDING → READY | REJECTED;
    QUARANTINED reserved for the V1.5 scanner. Soft delete via deletedAt.
  - READY authority: the server, always. markFileReady moves a row only from
    PENDING, so finalising twice returns the same file and a concurrent pair
    produces one READY row. A browser that claims success without uploading gets
    a refusal and the row stays PENDING.
  - Failure handling: validation failure → REJECTED with the reason, object
    deleted, and it cannot be resurrected. A missing object → refusal with the
    row left PENDING, so a retry can still succeed.
  - Cleanup: sweepStalePendingUploads reaps PENDING rows older than 24 hours,
    matching the bucket's own quarantine/ lifecycle rule. It is a function, not 
    a schedule — nothing calls it yet, and the cron arrives with Phase 8A's
    scheduled jobs. Stated as a deferral rather than implied to exist.

  Custom builder

  - Real upload: the same five steps. A selected file is authorised, PUT to
    storage and verified; the payload now carries designFileIds, and the request
    service re-reads each one scoped to the owner and requires READY. This 
    closes Phase 7's limitation: condition 7 of §2.3 is genuinely answerable, so
    a logo on a simple configuration is a fixed price again rather than
    automatically a quote.
  - PNG/JPG: a server-generated JPEG preview, EXIF-rotated and stripped, served
    from a short-lived signed URL. Never a blob of the uploaded file — an E2E
    test asserts the thumbnail's source.
  - SVG/PDF: file card, no preview, with the existing "preview unavailable for
    this file type" note (ADR-011).
  - Removal/retry: removal tells the server too, and is refused for a file a
    submitted request already carries. A failed upload shows the server's reason
    and a Réessayer button. Status is announced in a live region.

  Image processing

  - Processing: sharp, in lib/media/raster.ts — the only decoder in the project.
    Metadata inspection, orientation normalisation, flattening onto white, and
    a 640 px JPEG preview.
  - Dimension/resource limits: limitInputPixels at 40 MP, failOn: 'error',
    single frame, plus the domain's edge and area caps. One real defect found
    here: metadata() parses the header without proving the image decodes, so a
    PNG with a valid header and a corrupt body passed validation and only failed
    later at preview time. The pixels are now actually read, and there is a
    regression test using the exact file that slipped through.

  Privacy

  - Access model: private bucket, no public path, keys unguessable and never
    returned to a client. quarantine/ → designs/ promotion means an unvalidated
    object is never reachable normally.
  - Signed/private access: GET signed per request after an authorisation check,
    five-minute lifetime. A customer's own file always carries attachment +
    application/octet-stream, enforced at the provider, so a leaked URL
    downloads rather than renders. A generated preview is signed without them,
    because its bytes are a JPEG the server re-encoded.

  Security tests

  - Metadata spoofing: PDF-as-PNG, executable-as-PNG, HTML-as-SVG, corrupt
    image, header-valid/body-corrupt PNG, unsupported type, zero-byte,
    oversized, body larger than signed, body typed differently from signed.
  - Authorization: guest A cannot finalise, view, download or discard guest B's
    file; user A cannot read user B's; invented ids refused; no storage key ever
    reaches the client; a request naming another guest's file is refused.
  - Lifecycle: never READY without bytes; idempotent finalisation; concurrent
    finalisation; rejected file cannot be resurrected; deleted file cannot be
    reused; stale sweep; a recent incomplete upload is left alone.
  - Storage failures: object vanishing between PUT and completion, and a failed
    upload retried to success.
  - SVG security: script and handler refused, XXE refused, no object left behind
    on rejection, and an accepted SVG served with all three attachment headers.

  Tests

  Counts from the final run.

  - Unit: 149 (40 new in design-file-domain.test.ts).
  - Integration: 177 against PostgreSQL and a real storage adapter (37 new in
    design-file.test.ts). Also replayed onto a fresh database with the seed run
    twice.
  - E2E: 81 passed, including 11 upload tests in the builder flow; a repeat pass
    at --repeat-each=3 ran 60/60 with no flakiness.
  - Architecture: domain probe 8 errors, component probe 5, generated-client
    probe 1 per layer, and a new storage-SDK probe — 2 per layer. That probe
    found a real gap: nothing had stopped a component importing
    @aws-sdk/client-s3 or sharp. lib/storage and lib/media are now the only
    directories permitted to.
  - Accessibility: axe clean at all five builder steps; 375 px overflow checks
    at every step.
  - Build: passes; /api/storage/local registered, catalogue still static with
    ISR.

  Documentation

  - Updated: docs/DECISIONS.md (ADR-024), docs/SECURITY.md (§6 amended — refuse
    rather than sanitise; the size-constant and real-decode notes),
    docs/ARCHITECTURE.md (the adapter as built, the pipeline diagram, file
    ownership), docs/DATABASE.md (ownerTokenHash, the check, the indexes, the
    lifecycle and the sweep), .env.example (the S3_* block it had already
    anticipated, plus local behaviour), tests/README.md (the new probe),
    tasks/CURRENT.md (TASK-013). docs/UX.md needed no change.

  Findings

  - Critical: none.
  - High: none.
  - Medium:
    a. The S3 adapter has never run against a bucket. No credentials and no R2
       account exist, so it is exercised by nothing but the type system and the
       build. Provisioning the bucket and doing one end-to-end upload is a
       launch prerequisite, not something a test here can stand in for.
    b. The sweep has no scheduler. sweepStalePendingUploads is tested but
       uncalled; the cron belongs with Phase 8A's scheduled jobs. Until then an
       abandoned row lives on, though the bucket's own 24-hour rule still takes
       the object.
    c. The presign endpoint is unauthenticated and unrated. A guest cookie plus
       a loop can create PENDING rows and objects; a per-owner ceiling of 40
       live files bounds it, but real rate limiting is Phase 15.
  - Low:
    a. npm audit reports 4 pre-existing high-severity advisories, all in
       Prisma's transitive deepmerge-ts and mysql2 (a driver this PostgreSQL
       project never loads). The offered fix downgrades Prisma 7 → 6. Not
       introduced by this phase; left alone deliberately.
    b. The SVG detector is text-based (ADR-024). An adversarially-written but
       technically-valid SVG could defeat it and be stored — it would still
       never be rendered, which is why the attachment and no-preview controls
       carry the weight.
    c. S3_BUCKET_PUBLIC is reserved and read by nothing; product photography
       needs the admin media UI, which is out of scope.
    d. Phase 9's roadmap entry also lists the design-on-product preview,
       URL-addressable steps, drafts and multi-item requests. This phase built
       the upload pipeline those depend on; they remain outstanding and are
       recorded in CURRENT.md.

  Phase 8 compatibility: no order or cart business rule was touched. Historical
  access uses the documented relationship — an order item points at a
  configuration, which points at its files — and is protected by two locks:
  customization_files.designFileId is ON DELETE RESTRICT, and discardFile
  refuses a file any configuration links. A test covers it. The Phase 8 files in
  the changed-file list were touched only by prettier.

  Phase 9 status

  - PASS

  Phase 10 readiness

  - YES
  - Quotes need an admin who can open a customer's artwork, and that artwork is
    now stored, verified, owned and reachable only through a short-lived
    authorised URL — with the one carry-over that the R2 bucket itself has still
    to be provisioned and proven with a single real upload.

  # Goal                                                                        
                                                                                
  Implement the complete V1 customer quote workflow:                            
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
  CUSTOM REQUEST                                                                
        ↓                                                                       
  Admin reviews request                                                         
        ↓                                                                       
  REQUEST / MESSAGE / NEEDS_INFO                                                
        ↓                                                                       
  Quote created                                                                 
        ↓                                                                       
  Quote sent                                                                    
        ↓                                                                       
  Customer reviews                                                              
        ↓                                                                       
  Accept / decline                                                              
        ↓                                                                       
  Exclusive quote-bound cart                                                    
        ↓                                                                       
  Phase 8 checkout/order pipeline                                               
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  The quote system must preserve the existing distinction:                      
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
  CustomRequest ≠ Quote ≠ Order                                                 
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  A quote is not an order.                                                      
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  # 1. Quote state machine                                                      
                                                                                
  Implement only the states defined by the existing business rules/schema.      
                                                                                
  Explicitly enforce valid transitions server-side.                             
                                                                                
  Pay particular attention to:                                                  
                                                                                
  * draft/preparation                                                           
  * sent                                                                        
  * accepted                                                                    
  * declined                                                                    
  * expired                                                                     
  * cancelled                                                                   
  * `NEEDS_INFO`                                                                
  * `MESSAGE`                                                                   
                                                                                
  Do not invent a second state machine.                                         
                                                                                
  ### Critical rule                                                             
                                                                                
  `NEEDS_INFO` is a request/quote workflow state only when the documented       
  blocking-information condition is met.                                        
                                                                                
  Ordinary conversation must remain `MESSAGE`.                                  
                                                                                
  Do not turn every customer message into `NEEDS_INFO`.                         
                                                                                
  Invalid transitions must fail closed.                                         
                                                                                
  Add transition tests for every documented state.                              
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  # 2. Quote versioning                                                         
                                                                                
  Quotes are versioned and become immutable once sent.                          
                                                                                
  Implement the documented version model.                                       
                                                                                
  Critical invariant:                                                           
                                                                                
  > A sent quote must never be mutated in place.                                
                                                                                
  If information changes after a quote has been sent:                           
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
  old quote                                                                     
     ↓                                                                          
  new quote version                                                             
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  not:                                                                          
                                                                                
  ```text                                                                       
  UPDATE old sent quote                                                         
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  Preserve the old ve

  em primitives.

  * 375                                                                         
  * 768                                                                         
  * 1024                                                                        
  * 1440

  * keyboard
