      439 +                                                              
      440 +export type AcceptFailure =                                   
      441 +  | { code: 'NOT_ACCEPTABLE' }                                
      442 +  | { code: 'ALREADY_ACCEPTED'; cartId: string }              
      443 +  | { code: 'NO_LINES' };                                     
      444 +                                                              
      445 +/**                                                           
      446 + * Accept a quote, atomically (7.6).                          
      447 + *                                                            
      448 + * One transaction: transition the quote; transition the reque
          +st; set                                                       
      449 + * `acceptanceValidUntil`; create the **quote-bound cart** wit
          +h every line                                                  
      450 + * mapped per 2.6.5, each carrying its `lockedUnitPrice`; writ
          +e both events.                                                
      451 + * The customer's standard cart is not touched (5A.2).        
      452 + *                                                            
      453 + * Idempotent and race-safe by the same technique Phase 8 used
          + for placement:                                               
      454 + * the transition is a conditional `updateMany` from the outst
          +anding statuses,                                              
      455 + * so of two simultaneous acceptances exactly one matches a ro
          +w. The loser finds                                            
      456 + * the quote already `ACCEPTED` and is handed the cart the win
          +ner created, which                                            
      457 + * is the same outcome it wanted.                             
      458 + */                                                           
      459 +export async function acceptQuote(input: {                    
      460 +  quoteId: string;                                            
      461 +  userId: string;                                             
      462 +  acceptanceValidUntil: Date;                                 
      463 +  /** Quote lines that become cart lines, already mapped by th
          +e service. */                                                 
      464 +  cartLines: readonly {                                       
      465 +    kind: 'CUSTOM' | 'FEE';                                   
      466 +    quoteItemId: string;                                      
      467 +    customizationConfigId: string | null;                     
      468 +    productId: string | null;                                 
      469 +    variantId: string | null;                                 
      470 +    nameSnapshot: string | null;                              
      471 +    lockedUnitPrice: number;                                  
      472 +    quantity: number;                                         
      473 +  }[];                                                        
      474 +  quoteValidUntil: Date;                                      
      475 +}): Promise<{ ok: true; cartId: string } | { ok: false; failur
          +e: AcceptFailure }> {                                         
      476 +  if (input.cartLines.length === 0) return { ok: false, failur
          +e: { code: 'NO_LINES' } };                                    
      477 +                                                              
      478 +  return prisma.$transaction(async (tx) => {                  
      479 +    const acceptedAt = new Date();                            
      480 +                                                              
      481 +    const claimed = await tx.quote.updateMany({               
      482 +      where: {                                                
      483 +        id: input.quoteId,                                    
      484 +        userId: input.userId,                                 
      485 +        status: { in: ['SENT', 'VIEWED'] },                   
      486 +        // The expiry check, in the query. A quote whose valid
          +ity lapsed between                                            
      487 +        // the page render and the click must not be acceptabl
          +e, and a check in                                             
      488 +        // JavaScript would have been made before the row was 
          +locked.                                                       
      489 +        validUntil: { gte: acceptedAt },                      
      490 +      },                                                      
      491 +      data: {                                                 
      492 +        status: 'ACCEPTED',                                   
      493 +        acceptedAt,                                           
      494 +        respondedAt: acceptedAt,                              
      495 +        acceptanceValidUntil: input.acceptanceValidUntil,     
      496 +      },                                                      
      497 +    });                                                       
      498 +                                                              
      499 +    if (claimed.count === 0) {                                
      500 +      // Either somebody else won the race, or it was never ac
          +ceptable. The                                                 
      501 +      // difference matters: the first is a success the caller
          + should be handed.                                            
      502 +      const existing = await tx.quote.findFirst({             
      503 +        where: { id: input.quoteId, userId: input.userId, stat
          +us: 'ACCEPTED' },                                             
      504 +        select: { cart: { select: { id: true } } },           
      505 +      });                                                     
      506 +                                                              
      507 +      if (existing?.cart) {                                   
      508 +        return { ok: false as const, failure: { code: 'ALREADY
          +_ACCEPTED' as const, cartId: existing.cart.id } };            
      509 +      }                                                       
      510 +                                                              
      511 +      return { ok: false as const, failure: { code: 'NOT_ACCEP
          +TABLE' as const } };                                          
      512 +    }                                                         
      513 +                                                              
      514 +    const quote = await tx.quote.findUniqueOrThrow({          
      515 +      where: { id: input.quoteId },                           
      516 +      select: { requestId: true, quoteNumber: true, request: {
          + select: { status: true } } },                                
      517 +    });                                                       
      518 +                                                              
      519 +    // 5A: an exclusive cart, bound to this quote, containing 
          +exactly its lines.                                            
      520 +    const cart = await tx.cart.create({                       
      521 +      data: {                                                 
      522 +        kind: 'QUOTE_BOUND',                                  
      523 +        status: 'ACTIVE',                                     
      524 +        userId: input.userId,                                 
      525 +        quoteId: input.quoteId,                               
      526 +        quoteValidUntil: input.acceptanceValidUntil,          
      527 +        items: {                                              
      528 +          create: input.cartLines.map((line, position) => ({  
      529 +            kind: line.kind,                                  
      530 +            productId: line.productId,                        
      531 +            variantId: line.variantId,                        
      532 +            customizationConfigId: line.customizationConfigId,
      533 +            quoteItemId: line.quoteItemId,                    
      534 +            nameSnapshot: line.nameSnapshot,                  
      535 +            lockedUnitPrice: line.lockedUnitPrice,            
      536 +            quantity: line.quantity,                          
      537 +            position,                                         
      538 +          })),                                                
      539 +        },                                                    
      540 +      },                                                      
      541 +      select: { id: true },                                   
      542 +    });                                                       
      543 +                                                              
      544 +    await tx.customRequest.update({                           
      545 +      where: { id: quote.requestId },                         
      546 +      data: { status: 'ACCEPTED', respondedAt: acceptedAt },  
      547 +    });                                                       
      548 +                                                              
      549 +    await tx.customRequestEvent.createMany({                  
      550 +      data: [                                                 
      551 +        {                                                     
      552 +          requestId: quote.requestId,                         
      553 +          type: 'STATUS_CHANGE',                              
      554 +          fromStatus: quote.request.status,                   
      555 +          toStatus: 'ACCEPTED',                               
      556 +          actorType: 'CUSTOMER',                              
      557 +          actorUserId: input.userId,                          
      558 +          message: `Devis ${quote.quoteNumber} accepté.`,     
      559 +          isCustomerVisible: true,                            
      560 +        },                                                    
      561 +      ],                                                      
      562 +    });                                                       
      563 +                                                              
      564 +    return { ok: true as const, cartId: cart.id };            
      565 +  });                                                         
      566 +}                                                             
      567 +                                                              
      568 +/** 7.8: rejection may capture a reason. The quote is never de
          +leted. */                                                     
      569 +export async function declineQuote(input: {                   
      570 +  quoteId: string;                                            
      571 +  userId: string;                                             
      572 +  reason: string | null;                                      
      573 +}): Promise<boolean> {                                        
      574 +  return prisma.$transaction(async (tx) => {                  
      575 +    const rejectedAt = new Date();                            
      576 +                                                              
      577 +    const declined = await tx.quote.updateMany({              
      578 +      where: { id: input.quoteId, userId: input.userId, status
          +: { in: ['SENT', 'VIEWED'] } },                               
      579 +      data: {                                                 
      580 +        status: 'REJECTED',                                   
      581 +        rejectedAt,                                           
      582 +        respondedAt: rejectedAt,                              
      583 +        rejectionReason: input.reason,                        
      584 +      },                                                      
      585 +    });                                                       
      586 +                                                              
      587 +    if (declined.count === 0) return false;                   
      588 +                                                              
      589 +    const quote = await tx.quote.findUniqueOrThrow({          
      590 +      where: { id: input.quoteId },                           
      591 +      select: { requestId: true, quoteNumber: true, request: {
          + select: { status: true } } },                                
      592 +    });                                                       
      593 +                                                              
      594 +    await tx.customRequest.update({                           
      595 +      where: { id: quote.requestId },                         
      596 +      data: { status: 'REJECTED', respondedAt: rejectedAt },  
      597 +    });                                                       
      598 +                                                              
      599 +    await tx.customRequestEvent.create({                      
      600 +      data: {                                                 
      601 +        requestId: quote.requestId,                           
      602 +        type: 'STATUS_CHANGE',                                
      603 +        fromStatus: quote.request.status,                     
      604 +        toStatus: 'REJECTED',                                 
      605 +        actorType: 'CUSTOMER',                                
      606 +        actorUserId: input.userId,                            
      607 +        message: input.reason                                 
      608 +          ? `Devis ${quote.quoteNumber} refusé : ${input.reaso
          +n}`                                                           
      609 +          : `Devis ${quote.quoteNumber} refusé.`,             
      610 +        isCustomerVisible: true,                              
      611 +      },                                                      
      612 +    });                                                       
      613 +                                                              
      614 +    return true;                                              
      615 +  });                                                         
      616 +}                                                             
      617 +                                                              
      618 +/* -----------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------                                              
      619 + * Expiry sweeps (7.4, 7.10.2)                                
      620 + * -----------------------------------------------------------
          +------------- */                                              
      621 +                                                              
      622 +/**                                                           
      623 + * Expire outstanding quotes past their validity.             
      624 + *                                                            
      625 + * The scheduled half of 7.4. The lazy half lives in `domain/q
          +uote`, so a                                                   
      626 + * stalled job cannot make an expired quote acceptable — this 
          +only makes the                                                
      627 + * stored rows agree with what every reader already computes. 
      628 + */                                                           
      629 +export async function expireOutstandingQuotes(now: Date = new 
          +Date()): Promise<number> {                                    
      630 +  const expired = await prisma.quote.updateMany({             
      631 +    where: { status: { in: ['SENT', 'VIEWED'] }, validUntil: {
          + lt: now } },                                                 
      632 +    data: { status: 'EXPIRED' },                              
      633 +  });                                                         
      634 +                                                              
      635 +  return expired.count;                                       
      636 +}                                                             
      637 +                                                              
      638 +/** 7.10.2: an accepted quote's cart is invalidated once accep
          +tance lapses. */                                              
      639 +export async function invalidateLapsedQuoteCarts(now: Date = n
          +ew Date()): Promise<number> {                                 
      640 +  const invalidated = await prisma.cart.updateMany({          
      641 +    where: {                                                  
      642 +      kind: 'QUOTE_BOUND',                                    
      643 +      status: 'ACTIVE',                                       
      644 +      quoteValidUntil: { lt: now },                           
      645 +    },                                                        
      646 +    data: { status: 'INVALIDATED' },                          
      647 +  });                                                         
      648 +                                                              
      649 +  return invalidated.count;                                   
      650 +}                                                             
      651 +                                                              
      652 +export type RequestStatusUpdate = {                           
      653 +  requestId: string;                                          
      654 +  from: RequestStatus;                                        
      655 +  to: RequestStatus;                                          
      656 +  actorUserId: string | null;                                 
      657 +  actorType: 'CUSTOMER' | 'ADMIN' | 'SYSTEM';                 
      658 +  needsInfoReason?: string | null;                            
      659 +  message?: string | null;                                    
      660 +};                                                            
      661 +                                                              
      662 +/**                                                           
      663 + * Move a request, conditionally on it still being where the c
          +aller thought.                                                
      664 + *                                                            
      665 + * 6.4: no code sets `status` without going through the transi
          +tion function, and                                            
      666 + * every transition writes an event. The `status: from` filter
          + is what makes a                                              
      667 + * concurrent second transition fail rather than overwrite.   
      668 + */                                                           
      669 +export async function applyRequestTransition(                 
      670 +  input: RequestStatusUpdate,                                 
      671 +): Promise<boolean> {                                         
      672 +  return prisma.$transaction(async (tx) => {                  
      673 +    const moved = await tx.customRequest.updateMany({         
      674 +      where: { id: input.requestId, status: input.from },     
      675 +      data: {                                                 
      676 +        status: input.to,                                     
      677 +        needsInfoReason: input.to === 'NEEDS_INFO' ? (input.ne
          +edsInfoReason ?? null) : null,                                
      678 +        ...(input.to === 'IN_REVIEW' ? { reviewedAt: new Date(
          +) } : {}),                                                    
      679 +      },                                                      
      680 +    });                                                       
      681 +                                                              
      682 +    if (moved.count === 0) return false;                      
      683 +                                                              
      684 +    await tx.customRequestEvent.create({                      
      685 +      data: {                                                 
      686 +        requestId: input.requestId,                           
      687 +        type: 'STATUS_CHANGE',                                
      688 +        fromStatus: input.from,                               
      689 +        toStatus: input.to,                                   
      690 +        actorType: input.actorType,                           
      691 +        actorUserId: input.actorUserId,                       
      692 +        message: input.message ?? input.needsInfoReason ?? nul
          +l,                                                            
      693 +        isCustomerVisible: true,                              
      694 +      },                                                      
      695 +    });                                                       
      696 +                                                              
      697 +    return true;                                              
      698 +  });                                                         
      699 +}                                                             
      700 +                                                              
      701 +/** 6.7.2: a message is a message. It never touches the status
          +. */                                                          
      702 +export async function addRequestMessage(input: {              
      703 +  requestId: string;                                          
      704 +  actorType: 'CUSTOMER' | 'ADMIN';                            
      705 +  actorUserId: string | null;                                 
      706 +  message: string;                                            
      707 +  internal: boolean;                                          
      708 +}): Promise<void> {                                           
      709 +  await prisma.customRequestEvent.create({                    
      710 +    data: {                                                   
      711 +      requestId: input.requestId,                             
      712 +      type: input.internal ? 'INTERNAL_NOTE' : 'MESSAGE',     
      713 +      actorType: input.actorType,                             
      714 +      actorUserId: input.actorUserId,                         
      715 +      message: input.message,                                 
      716 +      // An internal note never leaves the admin (DATABASE.md 
          +section 15).                                                  
      717 +      isCustomerVisible: !input.internal,                     
      718 +    },                                                        
      719 +  });                                                         
      720 +}

        2  
        3  ## Current Sprint / Focus
        4  
        5 +**Phase 10 — The quote lifecycle. Complete.**                 
        6 +                                                              
        7 +A custom request now becomes a priced quote, and an accepted q
          +uote becomes a commitment the                                 
        8 +business can be held to. The three things that make that safe 
          +are each enforced in exactly one                              
        9 +place: **a sent quote is immutable** (a conditional `UPDATE` o
          +n `status = 'DRAFT'`), **only the                             
       10 +owning customer accepts** (ownership filtered in the query, th
          +en re-checked over the loaded row),                           
       11 +and **acceptance is one transaction** whose conditional claim 
          +makes two simultaneous acceptances                            
       12 +produce exactly one cart.                                     
       13 +                                                              
       14 +**No total, at any point, comes from a browser.** The admin se
          +nds lines; the server derives the                             
       15 +subtotal, the discount, the shipping, the grand total and the 
          +deposit — and there is no field in                            
       16 +any schema here for one to arrive in.                         
       17 +                                                              
       18 +---                                                           
       19 +                                                              
       20 +## TASK-014: Phase 10 — Quote lifecycle                       
       21 +                                                              
       22 +Status: DONE                                                  
       23 +                                                              
       24 +Related docs: `docs/BUSINESS-RULES.md` sections 2.6, 5A, 6 and
          + 7, `docs/DATABASE.md` sections 10

          +ions 4 and 7, ADR-015, ADR-016,                               
       26 +ADR-023, ADR-025

       53 +### Three things worth recording                              
       54 +                                                              
       55 +**The quote sequence yields a base, not a number.** `Q-1042-v2
          +` is version 2 of the family                                  
       56 +`1042`, so a revision reuses its predecessor's base and only t
          +he suffix moves. A sequence per                               
       57 +quote would have given version 2 an unrelated number and made 
          +the version history unreadable.

          +constrained to quantity 1, no                                 
       61 +configuration and `requiresProduction = false`, and embroidery
          + is none of those. The constraints                            
       62 +were right and the table was too narrow.                      
       63 +

          +ase 10 found it.** The migration                              
       65 +created `next_request_number()`; the repository still read `MA
          +X(request_number)`. Nothing had ever                          
       66 +created two requests at once, so nothing noticed until this ph
          +ase's suite did — and then it                                 
       67 +collided on the unique index, exactly as the Phase 7 audit pre

       69 +                                                              
       70 +### The security boundary                                     
       71 +                                                              
       72 +The draft payload has **no subtotal, no discount, no shipping,
          + no grand total, no deposit, no                               
       73 +status, no quote number and no version**. The one identifier i
          +t may carry, `requestItemId`, is                              
       74 +checked against *that request's* items before it is stored, so
          + a quote line cannot be pointed at                            
       75 +another customer's configuration.                             
       76 +

          +arially: an update of a sent quote,

       79 +simultaneous acceptances, a coupon forced onto a quote-bound c

          +frozen configuration into a fresh                             
       88 +  one is the missing half, and it belongs with the multi-item

● PHASE 10 RESULT

  Quote state machine

  - States: DRAFT, SENT, VIEWED, ACCEPTED, REJECTED, EXPIRED, CANCELLED,
    SUPERSEDED. Five are terminal.
  - Valid transitions: exactly BUSINESS-RULES.md 7.1, in
    domain/quote/QUOTE_TRANSITIONS. Enforcement is not a JavaScript check — it
    is a conditional UPDATE … WHERE status IN (…) per change, because a check
    made before the row is locked would let two simultaneous acceptances both
    pass. The IN list is built by statusesThatCanReach(to), which reads the same
    table, so the rule lives in one place and the query that enforces it cannot
    drift from it. No code path sets a quote status unconditionally. 64 unit
    assertions enumerate every ordered pair of statuses against the table.
  - NEEDS_INFO vs MESSAGE: two separate actions with two separate forms.
    postRequestMessage contains no transition at all — there is no code path
    from it to a status — and eventChangesStatus is true only for STATUS_CHANGE.
    NEEDS_INFO is a transition, refused without a reason
    (MISSING_NEEDS_INFO_REASON), permitted only from SUBMITTED, IN_REVIEW or
    QUOTED (6.7.4), and cleared when the admin returns the request to IN_REVIEW.

  Versioning

  - Version strategy: (requestId, version) is unique in the database; version is
    previous + 1 inside the create transaction. The number is Q-<base> /
    Q-<base>-v<n>, where the base comes from next_quote_base() over
    quote_number_seq and a revision reuses its predecessor's base — so Q-1042
    and Q-1042-v2 read as one conversation.
  - Sent immutability: updateDraftQuote is conditional on status: 'DRAFT', so a
    quote sent between the service check and the write still refuses. A test
    mutates a sent quote through the service and asserts lines, kinds, labels,
    unit prices, totals, deposit, validity and customer message are all
    byte-identical afterwards.
  - Customization freeze: written in the same transaction as the transition to
    SENT (7.2.1) — frozenAt plus productNameSnapshot, placementLabel,
    embroiderySizeLabel, fontLabel, threadColorNames. The freeze filters on
    frozenAt: null, so version 2 cannot rewrite what version 1 recorded; a test
    asserts the timestamp is unchanged after a second send. Another renames and 
    deactivates the live placement after sending and asserts the quote still
    shows the original name.

  Pricing

  - Server authority: the draft schema has no subtotal, discountTotal,
    shippingTotal, grandTotal, depositRequired, status, quoteNumber or version —
    absent, so a payload carrying them parses without them. A test posts all
    eight and asserts the stored quote is the server's numbers and DRAFT. The
    one identifier accepted, requestItemId, is validated against that request's
    items; a foreign one is refused.
  - Deposit: depositFor over the settings percentage in basis points; asserted
    equal to applyBasisPoints(grandTotal, custom_orders.depositPercent) read
    from the database. Copied, never recomputed, at placement (5.5.1).
  - Shipping: SHIPPING lines are the shipping total and supersede rate
    resolution entirely — shippingRateId stays null and the label is "Livraison
    devisée", both asserted on a placed order.
  - Historical snapshot: summariseConfigurations reads the snapshot whenever a
    configuration is frozen and never falls back to the live row.

  Quote

  - Quote number: PostgreSQL sequence, not MAX(), not a row count, not a
    timestamp. Two quotes get distinct numbers; the unique index is the last
    line of defence.
  - Validity: 14 days and 7 days, read from custom_orders.quoteValidityDays and
    custom_orders.acceptanceValidityDays via loadQuoteSettings(). A missing key
    throws rather than defaulting. Boundaries are deliberate and tested: valid
    up to and including validUntil, expired one millisecond later.
  - Sending: one transaction — supersede the previous outstanding quote,
    transition, freeze, move the request to QUOTED, write QUOTE_SENT and
    STATUS_CHANGE. validateSendable is re-checked at send time, not only at
    drafting.
  - Expiration: enforced twice. expireOutstandingQuotes is the sweep;
    effectiveQuoteStatus is the lazy check every read uses. A test backdates a
    quote without touching its status and asserts the customer sees "expired"
    while the stored row still says otherwise.

  Acceptance

  - Transaction: one — claim the quote, set acceptanceValidUntil, create the
    quote-bound cart with every mapped line and its lockedUnitPrice, move the
    request, write the event.
  - Idempotency: a second acceptance returns the first cart with
    alreadyAccepted: true; carts.quoteId is unique, so a second cart is
    impossible.
  - Concurrency: three simultaneous acceptances produce one cart and one
    ACCEPTED quote, asserted.
  - Quote-bound cart: contains exactly the priced lines. SHIPPING and DISCOUNT
    are excluded — Phase 8 reads them from the quote, so mapping them would
    charge them twice.

  Admin

  - Request workspace: /admin/requests queue ordered by who is holding the work,
    and /admin/requests/[requestNumber] with the configuration, the customer,
    the full timeline including internal notes, and the version history.
  - Artwork: resolved through the Phase 9 boundary — a five-minute signed URL,
    obtained by the request the file is attached to (staff are not owners), with
    an audit-log row per staff access. No storage key is rendered.
  - Quote editor: lines only. It computes no totals: the admin saves, the server
    prices, and the customer preview beside it is the server's arithmetic.
  - Messages: a message form and a separate needs-info form, plus an
    internal-note switch that writes INTERNAL_NOTE.

  Customer

  - Quote page: number, version, effective status, expiry, itemised lines,
    subtotal, discount, delivery, total, deposit, the workshop's message, the
    configuration summary and the artwork.
  - Accept: sends the quote number and nothing else; lands on the quote-bound
    checkout.
  - Decline: confirmed, with an optional reason recorded verbatim.
  - Artwork: same signed-URL path, scoped by the request's ownership, not
    audit-logged (a customer reading their own is the expected case).

  Security

  - Authorization: requireCustomer / requireStaff over domain/permissions; no
    Better Auth admin plugin. Ownership is filtered in the query, so a foreign
    quote number is "not found", never "not yours". mayRespondToQuote refuses
    staff explicitly and requires a verified address.
  - Immutability: conditional UPDATEs, not UI state. Proven by mutating through
    the service.
  - Cart escape: refused for adding (lands in the standard cart instead),
    quantity change, removal, coupon (check constraint), conversion to a
    standard cart (check constraint), foreign access, and checkout past the
    acceptance window.
  - Money tampering: no money field exists in any Phase 10 payload.
  - Concurrency: conditional claims plus a partial unique index; a raw insert of
    a second outstanding quote is rejected by the database.

  Tests

  - Unit: 65 in tests/unit/quote-domain.test.ts — both state machines enumerated
    exhaustively, totals, sign rules, sendability, expiry boundaries,
    numbering, permissions.
  - Integration: 59 in tests/integration/quote.test.ts, adversarial throughout,
    against real PostgreSQL. Plus the Phase 8 checkout regression suite
    unchanged.
  - E2E: 3 in tests/e2e/quote.spec.ts — the whole journey through the real
    interfaces, decline with a reason, and 375px.
  - Architecture: domain probe 8 errors, component probe 5, generated-client
    probe 1 per layer, storage-SDK probe 2 per layer. All exit 1.
  - Accessibility: axe clean on the customer quote page, the quote-bound
    checkout, /login and /register.
  - Build: production build passes. Final counts: 543 vitest across 18 files, 84
    Playwright across three projects. Migrations replayed onto an empty
    database, seed run twice, integration suite run against it, migrate diff
    reports no difference.

  Documentation

  - Updated: tasks/CURRENT.md (Phase 10 record and next-phase list),
    docs/ROADMAP.md (Phase 10 marked done with the four deferred items),
    docs/DECISIONS.md (ADR-025), docs/BUSINESS-RULES.md 2.6.5, docs/DATABASE.md
    sections 1 and 10, docs/SECURITY.md sections 7 and the signed-URL table,
    docs/ARCHITECTURE.md section 8, docs/UX.md section 2.

  Findings

  - Critical: none.
  - High: none.
  - Medium (fixed): nextRequestNumber still read MAX(request_number). Phase 8's
    migration created next_request_number() and tasks/CURRENT.md recorded the
    retrofit as shipped, but the call site was never switched. Concurrent
    request creation collided on the unique index — exactly the race the Phase 7
    audit predicted. Now uses the sequence.
  - Medium (fixed): placeOrderFromCart never checked the acceptance window.
    loadQuoteTotals returned status and acceptanceValidUntil and neither was
    read, so a lapsed quote-bound cart would have checked out whenever the
    (unscheduled) sweep had not run. 7.10.2 requires a lazy check; it now exists
    and invalidates the cart on refusal.
  - Medium (fixed): the domain transition table was unused by production code —
    the repository restated it as SQL predicates. Correct but duplicated,
    against section 16. The predicates are now built from the table.
  - Medium (open, unproven): an unidentified single-test failure in the full
    vitest suite, seen twice in ~30 runs, both times in the first run following
    a Playwright run. I could not capture which test, and it never reproduced
    under instrumentation (11 targeted attempts). The most plausible cause is
    residue from the E2E suite in the shared database; a beforeAll/afterAll
    purge of the E2E accounts and their requests, quotes and carts was added,
    and three deliberate Playwright→vitest cycles since have been clean. This is
    a mitigation, not a proven fix.
  - Low (fixed): sendQuote caught every exception and reported it as "another
    quote is outstanding". Narrowed to P2002/23505; anything else rethrows.
  - Low (fixed): pre-existing schema drift — design_files' owner-token index was
    named one thing by the Phase 9 migration and another by the Prisma schema,
    so a clean replay reported a difference. Named explicitly.
  - Low (open): clone-on-edit (7.2.2) has no implementation, because no code
    path edits a configuration at all. A frozen configuration is safe today by
    absence rather than by refusal; the guard must be written when the
    multi-item builder adds an edit path.
  - Low (open): "Request an updated quote" (7.9, 7.10.3) offers a new request
    rather than a clone of the frozen configuration. The page says so plainly;
    nothing claims otherwise.

  Phase 10 status

  - PASS

  Phase 11 readiness

  - YES — the quote lifecycle, its two expiries, acceptance and the quote-bound
    checkout are implemented and proven by database-backed tests, and the four
    remaining Medium/Low items are either fixed or explicitly recorded as
    deferred work that Phase 11 and Phase 8A already own.

❯ # PHASE 11 — CUSTOMER PORTAL                                                  
                                                                                
  Phase 10 is approved. Continue from the current repository state.             
                                                                                
  Do NOT rebuild previous phases. Do NOT modify working Phase 8–10 business     
  logic unless required for a concrete integration/security issue.              
                                                                                
  ## Goal                                                                       
                                                                                
  Build the V1 authenticated customer portal that gives customers a secure      
  place to manage:                                                              
                                                                                
  * profile/account information                                                 
  * custom requests                                                             
  * quotes                                                                      
  * orders                                                                      
  * order details/status                                                        
  * messages relevant to their requests/quotes                                  
  * authorized artwork/design files where applicable                            
                                                                                
  The portal must consume the existing domain/services/repositories from Phases 
  7–10 rather than duplicating business logic.                                  
                                                                                
  ## READ FIRST                                                                 
                                                                                
  Before coding, read only the relevant sections of:                            
                                                                                
  * `docs/CURRENT.md`                                                           
  * `docs/ROADMAP.md`                                                           
  * `docs/PRODUCT.md`                                                           
  * `docs/UX.md`                                                                
  * `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`                                                      
  * `docs/DATABASE.md`                                                          
  * `docs/BUSINESS-RULES.md`                                                    
  * `docs/SECURITY.md`                                                          
  * `docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`                                                     
  * `docs/DECISIONS.md`                                                         
                                                                                
  Then inspect the actual implementations for:                                  
                                                                                
  * Phase 3 authentication/session handling                                     
  * Phase 4 design system                                                       
  * Phase 6 catalogue                                                           
  * Phase 7 custom requests                                                     
  * Phase 8 cart/checkout/orders                                                
  * Phase 9 design-file authorization                                           
  * Phase 10 requests/quotes/quote-bound carts                                  
                                                                                
  Do not assume documentation matches code. Follow the current implementation.  
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  # 1. CUSTOMER AUTHORIZATION — CRITICAL                                        
                                                                                
  Every portal query must be scoped to the authenticated customer.              
                                                                                
  Never trust:                                                                  
                                                                                
  * URL IDs                                                                     
  * request IDs                                                                 
  * quote IDs                                                                   
  * order IDs                                                                   
  * design-file IDs                                                             
  * query parameters                                                            
  * client-provided user IDs                                                    
                                                                                
  The authenticated actor must determine ownership.                             
                                                                                
  A customer attempting to access another customer's:                           
                                                                                
  * request                                                                     
  * quote                                                                       
  * order                                                                       
  * artwork                                                                     
  * messages                                                                    
                                                                                
  must receive the same safe "not found / unavailable" behavior as a            
  nonexistent resource.                                                         
                                                                                
  Do not introduce a second authorization system.                               
                                                                                
  Use the existing domain permission/actor model.                               
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  # 2. PORTAL ROUTES                                                            
                                                                                
  Implement the V1 customer-facing portal using the existing                    
  storefront/application architecture.                                          
                                                                                
  At minimum:                                                                   
                                                                                
  * `/account`                                                                  
  * `/account/requests`                                                         
  * `/account/requests/[requestNumber]`                                         
  * `/account/quotes`                                                           
  * `/account/quotes/[quoteNumber]`                                             
  * `/account/orders`                                                           
  * `/account/orders/[orderNumber]`                                             
  * `/account/orders/[orderNumber]/...` only if genuinely useful                
                                                                                
  Use the existing Phase 10 quote page where possible rather than creating      
  duplicate quote behavior.                                                     
                                                                                
  If the project already has an appropriate route structure, preserve it.

        5 +**Phase 11 — The customer portal. Complete.**                 
        6 +                                                              
        7 +A customer now has a place to see everything the business hold
          +s for them: their requests, the                               
        8 +quotes they have been sent, their orders, and the artwork they
          + uploaded. The portal adds **no                               
        9 +business rules**. It composes Phases 7 to 10 — ordering from `
          +domain/request` and `domain/order`,                           
       10 +quote status from Phase 10's own view, artwork from the Phase 
          +9 boundary — so there is nowhere in                           
       11 +it for a second version of a rule to live.                    
       12 +                                                              
       13 +The one thing it does add is a boundary, and it is enforced id
          +entically everywhere: **every read                            
       14 +is scoped by `userId` inside the query**, and somebody else's 
          +identifier produces exactly what a                            
       15 +made-up one produces.

          +son unrelated to rollback. Phase 10's

● Phase 11 is complete and validated.

  PHASE 11 RESULT

  Portal

  - Routes: /account, /account/requests, /account/requests/[requestNumber],
    /account/orders, /account/orders/[orderNumber], /account/profile — added
    this phase; /account/quotes and /account/quotes/[quoteNumber] preserved from
    Phase 10 and unwrapped so the new /account layout supplies the shell. One
    guard in the route-group layout, plus a guard in every page and every
    action, because a layout protects what is rendered and a server action is
    reachable without rendering one.
  - Account dashboard: the triage screen UX.md section 8 specifies, in its
    priority order — quotes awaiting a response (soonest expiry first), orders
    in flight, requests under review, recent orders, then the ways out. No
    statistic anywhere on it. Ordering comes from
    domain/request.isWaitingOnCustomer and a new domain/order.isOrderInFlight,
    not from a page.
  - Requests: number, status, submitted date, configuration read from the frozen
    snapshots, quantity, instructions, artwork, customer-visible timeline, the
    quotes it has produced, and the reply box. A blocked request leads with the
    reason verbatim.
  - Quotes: unchanged Phase 10 pages. No acceptance or decline logic was
    duplicated.
  - Orders: list and detail, both entirely from snapshots, with payment
    position, address, tracking and the status timeline.

  Messaging

  - Customer messages: postCustomerMessage — a separate service function from
    the admin's, resolving the request through findRequestForOwner so ownership
    is a query filter. It writes a MESSAGE with actorType: 'CUSTOMER'.
  - NEEDS_INFO: shown verbatim, with the reply box relabelled "Votre réponse"
    and copy explaining that the workshop reviews the answer. Answering does not
    clear the block — 6.7.3 gives that judgement to the admin — asserted at the
    service and in journey B.
  - Internal notes protection: the customer cannot create one, because
    postCustomerMessage has no internal argument. Reading is filtered in the
    query (isCustomerVisible: true, type: { not: 'INTERNAL_NOTE' }), so an
    internal note is never fetched rather than hidden. A test smuggles { 
    internal: true, actorType: 'ADMIN' } past the type system and asserts the
    row written is a customer MESSAGE.

  Authorization

  - Ownership enforcement: every portal read takes a userId that came from the
    session and filters on it inside the query. There is no variant of any
    portal function that accepts an actor plus a target id.
  - IDOR protection: a foreign identifier and a fabricated one produce the same
    answer, asserted by comparing them directly at the service level and by
    asserting 404 for both in journey E.
  - Artwork access: the Phase 9 boundary, unchanged — a five-minute signed URL
    resolved server-side, authorised by the request the file is attached to. No
    storage key reaches a DTO (asserted). Deleted, quarantined and not-READY
    files are refused. A customer reading their own is not audit-logged; a staff
    read still is (12.5).

  Quotes

  - Phase 10 integration: the portal links to the existing pages and calls the
    existing services. acceptQuoteAsOwner and declineQuoteAsOwner are untouched.
  - Acceptance: still one transaction, still conditional, still idempotent. An
    integration test asserts acceptance from the portal produces exactly one
    QUOTE_BOUND cart owned by the accepting customer.
  - Quote-bound checkout: unchanged Phase 8 path. Journey C drives portal →
    quote → accept → /checkout/quote/[cartId], then returns to the quote page
    and asserts it now offers "Finaliser ma commande" and no longer offers
    acceptance.

  Orders

  - Historical snapshots: nameSnapshot, variantLabelSnapshot, skuSnapshot,
    imageKeySnapshot, wilayaNameSnapshot, shippingRateLabel and the stored
    totals. A test renames the product and raises its price after placement and
    asserts the order view is byte-identical.
  - Order access: getCustomerOrder calls findOrderForViewer with no guest token
    — inside the account area, owning the row is the only thing that grants
    access. A test gives an order a guest token and confirms another customer
    still gets null.

  Security

  - Critical tests: all eighteen cases in the brief, at the service and
    repository level rather than the page — unauthenticated access,
    cross-customer request/quote/order/artwork/messages, fabricated
    request/quote/order/design-file ids, internal-note leakage, admin-only data
    leakage, client status and ownership manipulation, acceptance through the
    Phase 10 service, snapshot-backed history, deleted artwork, and an
    unverified customer.
  - Money/state tampering: no portal payload carries money or a status. The
    reply action's schema has two fields; the accept and decline actions carry a
    number and nothing else.
  - Sensitive-data leakage: quotes.internalNotes, orders.internalNotes, internal
    status-history entries and INTERNAL_NOTE events are each asserted absent
    from the customer projections. The profile repository selects five fields,
    so no credential exists in the DTO to leak; the E2E asserts no $argon2 or
    session-token string appears in the page.

  UX

  - Responsive: no horizontal overflow on any of the five portal routes at 375,
    768, 1024 and 1440px, asserted. Lists are cards rather than tables, so there
    is one layout rather than two kept in step; the portal navigation is a
    deliberate horizontal scroller inside its own container.
  - Accessibility: axe reports no WCAG 2.1 A/AA violations on /account,
    /account/requests, /account/requests/[requestNumber], /account/orders,
    /account/orders/[orderNumber] or /account/profile. Navigation uses
    aria-current="page" rather than colour alone.
  - Empty/loading/error states: designed empty states with a way out on
    requests, orders, quotes and the overview; the reply form and both quote
    actions render their own error and success states; a lapsed acceptance
    window has a page of its own rather than a 404.

  Tests

  - Unit: 217 (three new in commerce-domain.test.ts — the status-label map
    covers the state machine exactly, and isOrderInFlight's boundaries).
  - Integration: 269 across 8 files, of which 33 are the new account.test.ts.
  - E2E: 94 Playwright tests across three projects, of which 10 are the new
    account.spec.ts covering journeys A–E plus the signed-out gate, the profile,
    and four viewports. Portal and quote suites repeated twice more, clean.
  - Architecture: domain probe 8 errors, component probe 5, generated-client
    probe 1 per layer, storage-SDK probe 2 per layer — all exit 1. Prisma is
    imported in exactly seven files: lib/db/client.ts and the six repositories.
  - Accessibility: as above.
  - Build: production build passes. Migrations replayed onto an empty database,
    seed run twice, the integration suite run against it, migrate diff reports
    no difference. Final counts: 579 vitest across 19 files, 94 Playwright.

  Documentation

  - Updated: tasks/CURRENT.md (Phase 11 record; the Phase 10 entry amended so
    the intermittent-test trail is kept rather than dropped), docs/ROADMAP.md
    (Phase 11 marked done with its three deferred items), docs/UX.md (section 2
    route status and section 8), docs/SECURITY.md (section 13, portal scoping).
    No ADR was added — the portal introduced no architectural decision, only
    composition.

  Findings

  - Critical: none.
  - High: none.
  - Medium (fixed): a request submitted by a signed-in customer was never
    attached to their account — persistCustomRequest left userId null for
    everyone, and a request was claimed only when priced. Invisible until a
    portal existed to look for it in; the customer's own request was missing
    from their own list.
  - Medium (fixed): the design system's ORDER_STATUS map carried seven labels
    including a PENDING the database has never had, against the eleven
    BUSINESS-RULES.md section 8 defines. The portal renders a stored status
    straight into that badge, so a real AWAITING_PAYMENT row would have printed
    nothing. Both maps are the eleven now, with a unit test tying the domain's
    labels to the state machine.
  - Medium (fixed) — the tracked Phase 10 intermittent failure is resolved. It
    was five shared-state test assertions, each measuring the whole database
    rather than the thing under test: checkout.test.ts counting every order,
    auth.test.ts counting every verification row and every user,
    custom-request.test.ts counting every request, and Phase 10's freeze test
    deactivating a seeded placement that other suites resolve. Confirmed by
    reverting the order-count fix and reproducing the failure, then restoring
    it. The full suite has since run more than twenty times clean, including
    both sequences that used to trigger it.
  - Low (fixed): the two Phase 11 E2E blocks shared a fixture e-mail prefix, so
    one block's teardown deleted the other's customer mid-test. Each block now
    owns its prefix.
  - Low (open): CLAUDE.md still opens with "Phase 0 complete… no application
    code exists", which has been wrong since Phase 1. Out of Phase 11's scope to
    rewrite, but it is the first thing a new session reads and it should be
    corrected deliberately.
  - Low (open, documented): no address book, no name or password editing, no
    guest-order claiming; order_status_history and payments are written by the
    admin workspace, which is Phase 12, so a customer's order timeline is one
    line and every payment card reads "non réglée".

  Phase 11 status

  - PASS

  Phase 12 readiness

  - YES — the portal is complete for V1, every read is ownership-scoped and
    proven so by database-backed adversarial tests, no Phase 8–10 behaviour
    regressed, and the one long-standing test-reliability defect carried into
    this phase has been reproduced, fixed and verified.

✻ Sautéed for 52m 23s · done 5:03 AM

❯ # PHASE 12 — ADMIN OPERATIONS & ORDER MANAGEMENT                              
                                                                                
  Phase 11 is approved: PASS.                                                   
                                                                                
  Continue from the current repository state.

  existing domain/services/repositories and the Phase 10 admin request          
  workspace.                                                                    
                                                                                
  ## Goal                                                                       
                                                                                
  Build the V1 admin/staff operational workspace needed to actually run the     
  embroidery business after a customer submits a request or places an order.    
                                                                                
  This phase should complete the operational side of:                           
                                                                                
  * custom request management                                                   
  * quote/order operational management                                          
  * customer directory                                                          
  * order status management                                                     
  * payment/deposit tracking

  * customer-visible communication

  Keep it operational and V1-focused.                                           
                                                                                
  Do NOT build analytics, production scheduling, carrier APIs, or other         
  explicitly deferred features.

  # 2. ADMIN AUTHORIZATION — CRITICAL                                           
                                                                                
  All admin/staff pages and actions must require the existing staff/admin       
  authorization model.                                                          
                                                                                
  Use:                                                                          
                                                                                
  * existing authenticated actor                                                
  * existing domain permissions                                                 
  * existing staff/admin guards                                                 
                                                                                
  Do NOT introduce Better Auth admin plugin.                                    
                                                                                
  Do NOT rely on hidden UI links.                                               
                                                                                
  Every server action must independently authorize the actor.                   
                                                                                
  Test:                                                                         
                                                                                
  * unauthenticated access                                                      
  * customer access to admin routes                                             
  * staff access                                                                
  * admin-only actions                                                          
  * fabricated IDs                                                              
  * foreign resources                                                           
                                                                                
  Avoid leaking resource existence to unauthorized actors.

       50 +  partial unique index that permits one outstanding proof per

       51 +  and the two queue indexes.                                  
       52 +                                                              
       53 +### Four things worth recording                               
       54 +                                                              
       55 +**The reservation state is derived from the history, not store
          +d.** ADR-022 leaves an order that                             
       56 +entered `AWAITING_PAYMENT` holding nothing until it is confirm
          +ed, while one that entered                                    
       57 +`AWAITING_CONFIRMATION` reserved at placement. Whether an orde
          +r currently holds a reservation is                            
       58 +therefore a function of `order_status_history` — which `DATABA
          +SE.md` section 8 already calls the                            
       59 +truth — and so is the status an `ON_HOLD` order was held from.
          + A column for either would have been                          
       60 +a second copy of a fact the append-only history carries exactl
          +y.                                                            
       61 +                                                              
       62 +**No admin payload carries a `from`.** The status a transition
          + starts at is read from the row.                              
       63 +A client that could name its own starting point could pick one
          + from which its target is legal, and                          
       64 +`DELIVERED → REFUNDED` is legal from `DELIVERED`. A test posts
          + `from: 'DELIVERED'` against an                               
       65 +order in `AWAITING_CONFIRMATION` and asserts nothing moves.   
       66 +                                                              
       67 +**A payment is refused against a stale balance.** The service 
          +passes the `amountPaid` its screen                            
       68 +was showing, and the repository writes only if the row still a
          +grees. Two payments recorded at the                           
       69 +same instant would otherwise both be applied to a balance that
          + had already moved; one is now                                
       70 +refused with "rechargez", and a test races them.              
       71 +                                                              
       72 +**The design system and the domain disagreed about one word.**
          + `ON_HOLD` was "En attente" in the                            
       73 +domain and "En pause" on the badge — and "en attente" already 
          +meant "waiting for your deposit" two                          
       74 +rows above it. Both are "En pause" now. An end-to-end test tha
          +t selects a status by its label is                            
       75 +what found it, which is the argument for driving the real cont
          +rols rather than calling the action.                          
       76 +                                                              
       77 +### The security boundary                                     
       78 +                                                              
       79 +Every admin action authorises independently — `requireStaff`, 
          +then the capability predicate over                            
       80 +the loaded actor — because the layout guards what is rendered 
          +and an action is reachable without                            
       81 +rendering anything. A signed-in customer is redirected home ra
          +ther than refused, so the workshop's                          
       82 +existence is not confirmed to them.

       84 +Thirty-eight integration tests are written against this, adver

          +nt payload, an amount beyond the                              
       86 +balance, a refund beyond what was taken, a negative payment in
          +serted straight into the table, two
