● Phase 12 is complete and validated.

  PHASE 12 RESULT

  Admin shell

  - Routes: /admin, /admin/requests, /admin/requests/[requestNumber],
    /admin/quotes, /admin/orders, /admin/orders/[orderNumber], /admin/customers,
    /admin/customers/[userId].
  - Navigation: five sections with aria-current="page" on the current one, the
    actor's name, and a sign-out that revokes the session server-side rather
    than clearing a cookie. Below 768px the row collapses behind a labelled
    disclosure with aria-expanded/aria-controls; the design system's tokens and
    primitives throughout, no second visual language.
  - Dashboard: seven operational queues — new requests (oldest first, because
    the response promise starts there), requests in review, outstanding quotes
    (soonest expiry first), accepted quotes, orders to confirm, deposits
    awaited, work in fulfilment. Every row links to where it is worked. No 
    revenue figure and no chart: UX.md's sketch has one, PRODUCT.md puts
    analytics in V2, and a test asserts the payload contains no such thing.

  Requests

  - Queue: the Phase 10 queue, now filterable by status through the URL so a
    filtered queue can be bookmarked. The status list is derived from
    REQUEST_TRANSITIONS, so it cannot offer one that does not exist.
  - Request detail: unchanged Phase 10 workspace — configuration, artwork
    through Phase 9 signed URLs, full timeline, quote versions.
  - Status operations: unchanged Phase 10 service; no transition logic was
    duplicated.
  - Messages: unchanged; the MESSAGE/NEEDS_INFO distinction is the existing
    domain's.
  - Internal notes: on requests, unchanged (INTERNAL_NOTE events); on orders,
    new — orders.internalNotes, absent from the customer projection's select
    entirely.
  - Quotes: created, edited and sent through the existing Phase 10 services.
    /admin/quotes is a queue that links back to each request; it is not a second
    editor.

  Customers

  - Directory: /admin/customers, searched by name or address through a GET form
    so a search is a URL. Bounded query, executed server-side.
  - Customer detail: account age, verification state, status, phone, and the
    orders, requests and quotes that belong to them.
  - Authorization: staff capability, not a relationship — 12.1 gives staff "view
    any customer's order". The projection selects five identity fields and no
    credential; sessions and accounts are not fetched, so there is nothing to
    leak. A staff id and a fabricated id both return null, because the query
    filters on role: 'CUSTOMER'.

  Orders

  - Order list: /admin/orders, filtered by status through the URL, showing the
    payment position and flagging a proof waiting to be looked at.
  - Order detail: snapshot-backed items with SKU, the totals, the shipping
    address and rate label, the payment ledger with who recorded each row, the
    proof history, the full status history including internal entries, links to
    the originating request, quote and customer, and the internal-notes editor.
  - Status transitions: validated by domain/order/state-machine with the 8.7
    payload, then applied by a conditional UPDATE on the status the server read.
    The workspace offers only the moves the machine allows — ON_HOLD releases
    to the status it was held from, DESIGN_REVIEW appears only where something
    is made — and a cancellation is behind a confirmation because it releases
    stock. Stock follows ADR-022: confirming reserves for an order that reserved
    nothing at placement, cancelling releases what is held, and neither happens
    twice.
  - History: order_status_history, append-only, with from, to, actor, timestamp,
    note and visibility, written in the same transaction as the status and its
    timestamp column.

  Payments

  - Deposit: the 8.10 loop end to end — the customer sends a transfer receipt,
    the workshop reviews it, accepts or refuses it with a reason the customer
    reads, and records a payment. Recording the payment is what moves
    paymentStatus; nothing sets it.
  - Payment methods: COD, bank transfer, CCP. No provider integrated.
  - Proof: a new order_payment_proofs table — attached to the order because
    8.10.2 has proof arrive before any payment exists — with a partial unique
    index permitting one outstanding proof per order, a check that a refusal
    states a reason, and ON DELETE RESTRICT on the file so evidence cannot be
    swept away.
  - Authorization: through the Phase 9 boundary. Five-minute signed URL; a
    customer read scoped to an order they own; a staff read by capability and
    audit-logged (12.5); no storage key in any DTO.

  Shipping

  - Address: the order's snapshot — wilayaNameSnapshot, commune, line, phone,
    notes — never re-read.
  - Shipping: shippingRateLabel as stored, so a quote order still reads
    "Livraison devisée" and no rate lookup happens.
  - Fulfilment: the order is one fulfilment unit (8.9). Carrier and tracking
    number are captured at SHIPPED and required by the state machine. No carrier
    API, no production scheduling, and no invented delivery estimate —
    estimatedDelivery* stay null because the lead time 4.5 needs has no settings
    key.

  Security

  - Admin authorization: existing actor, existing guards, existing
    domain/permissions. No Better Auth admin plugin. The layout guards
    rendering; every action calls requireStaff and then the capability predicate
    over the loaded actor. A customer is redirected home rather than refused,
    so the area's existence is not confirmed.
  - IDOR: a fabricated order number, proof id or customer id all return the same
    nothing a real-but-absent one does.
  - Money tampering: no admin schema has a field for a total. A payload carrying
    grandTotal, depositRequired, paymentStatus or amountPaid parses without
    them, asserted. An amount beyond the balance, a refund beyond what was
    taken, a non-integer and a negative row are each refused — the last by a
    check constraint as well.
  - Internal-note leakage: order notes and hold notes are asserted absent from
    the customer's projection and from their rendered page.
  - Payment/artwork authorization: a proof is invisible to another customer and
    signs no URL for them; a deleted or non-READY file is refused; staff access
    writes an audit row.

  UX

  - Responsive: no horizontal overflow on any of the five admin list routes at
    375, 768, 1024 and 1440px, asserted. Dense two-column workspaces collapse to
    one below md.
  - Accessibility: axe clean on all seven admin pages. Radio groups and selects
    are the design system's; the status filter is a nav with aria-current;
    cancelling and refusing a proof are modals with focus trapping.
  - Loading/error/empty states: every control renders its own error and success
    alert; every list has a designed empty state that distinguishes "nothing at
    all" from "nothing in this filter"; a terminal order says plainly that
    nothing can move it.

  Tests

  - Unit: 252 (35 new in admin-domain.test.ts — the transition table enumerated
    over every ordered pair, the 8.7 payloads, ON_HOLD's release rule, the
    payment rules, and the 12.1 capability table).
  - Integration: 307 across 9 files, of which 38 are the new admin.test.ts.
  - E2E: 105 Playwright tests across three projects, of which 11 are
    admin.spec.ts — journeys A to G, the signed-out gate, the customer
    directory, and four viewports. The full suite repeated twice more, clean.
  - Architecture: domain probe 8 errors, component probe 5, generated-client
    probe 1 per layer, storage-SDK probe 2 per layer — all exit 1. Prisma stays
    in lib/db and the repositories.
  - Accessibility: as above.
  - Build: production build passes. Migrations replayed onto an empty database,
    seed run twice, migrate diff reports no difference, integration suite run
    against the fresh database (307 pass). Final counts: 652 vitest across 21
    files, 105 Playwright.

  Documentation

  - Updated: CLAUDE.md (the stale "Phase 0 complete, no application code exists"
    opening replaced with what actually exists, what does not, and a pointer to
    tasks/CURRENT.md as the file that is kept current), tasks/CURRENT.md (Phase
    12 record), docs/ROADMAP.md (Phase 12 marked as operations-delivered, with
    the remaining half listed rather than dropped), docs/DATABASE.md (the new
    table, enum, constraints and indexes), docs/SECURITY.md (admin
    authorisation, the money boundary, proof-of-payment media), docs/UX.md
    (section 9, dashboard and fulfilment journeys). No ADR was added — the phase
    introduced no new architectural decision, only application of ADR-022 and
    section 8.

  Findings

  - Critical: none.
  - High: none.
  - Medium (fixed): ON_HOLD was labelled "En attente" in the domain and "En
    pause" on the badge, and "en attente" already meant "waiting for your
    deposit" in the same list. Two vocabularies for one status; both are "En
    pause" now.
  - Low (fixed): the order queue formatted a deposit with inline / 100
    arithmetic instead of the money primitive — a formatting bug in the making,
    since amounts are integer minor units.
  - Low (fixed): logDesignFileAccess recorded its context as requestId, which
    would have been a lie for a payment proof. It takes a typed context now.
  - Low (fixed, tests): two fixture races in this phase's own E2E file — a
    describe split across workers running afterAll twice and deleting the other
    worker's account, and a test reading a quote number from the shared admin
    queue. Fixed by making the block serial with disjoint prefixes, and by
    reading the number from the request's own workspace.
  - Low (open): depositWindowDays is seeded as a placeholder, so 8.10.5's "flag
    an order past the window" is not implemented — the queue orders by age
    instead. It needs the owner's number.
  - Low (open): a REFUND payment moves amountPaid but triggers no status change;
    wiring DELIVERED → REFUNDED to it needs a returns policy the business has
    not set.
  - Low (open, documented): the guest half of the customer directory, review
    moderation, gallery, content, settings, the audit-log viewer, admin dark
    mode and admin catalogue management remain — ROADMAP.md's Phase 12 entry is
    wider than this brief's, and both it and tasks/CURRENT.md now say exactly
    which parts are done.

  Phase 12 status

  - PASS

  Phase 13 readiness

  - YES — the operational admin is complete and proven by database-backed
    adversarial tests and seven end-to-end journeys, no Phase 7–11 behaviour
    regressed, and every deferred item is named in the documentation rather than
    left to be discovered; note that Phase 8A's notifications are the more
    pressing next step, since nothing the workshop now does tells a customer
    about it.

✻ Cooked for 4m 24s · done 9:15 AM

※ recap: Building STITCH, an embroidery ordering platform, phase by phase. Phase 12 (admin operations: dashboard, order status, payments, customer directory) is complete and validated — 652 unit/integration tests and 105 E2E tests pass. Next: await your go-ahead for Phase 8A or 13. (disable recaps in /config)

❯ # PHASE 8A — TRANSACTIONAL NOTIFICATIONS & EMAIL                              
                                                                                
  Phase 12 is PASS and the existing implementation is validated.                
                                                                                
  Do **not** start Phase 13 yet.                                                
                                                                                
  Implement **Phase 8A: Transactional Notifications & Email** only.             
                                                                                
  ## 1. Read only what you need                                                 
                                                                                
  Before coding, read:                                                          
                                                                                
  * `docs/PRODUCT.md`                                                           
  * `docs/BUSINESS-RULES.md`                                                    
  * `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`                                                      
  * `docs/DATABASE.md`                                                          
  * `docs/SECURITY.md`                                                          
  * `docs/ROADMAP.md`                                                           
  * `docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`                                                     
  * `tasks/CURRENT.md`                                                          
                                                                                
  Then inspect only the existing code relevant to:                              
                                                                                
  * Better Auth/email seams from Phase 3/3.1                                    
  * Phase 8 order creation/payment flow                                         
  * Phase 10 quote lifecycle                                                    
  * Phase 11 customer portal                                                    
  * Phase 12 admin request/order/payment operations                             
  * existing notification/email abstractions, if any                            
                                                                                
  Do not reread unrelated implementation files.                                 
                                                                                
  ## 2. Goal                                                                    
                                                                                
  Create a production-ready transactional email notification system for the     
  existing V1 workflows.                                                        
                                                                                
  The purpose is simple:                                                        
                                                                                
  > When an important customer-facing event happens, the customer receives the  
  appropriate transactional email.                                              
                                                                                
  Reuse existing domain/application services and existing snapshots/state       
  machines.                                                                     
                                                                                
  Do NOT duplicate business logic inside the email system.                      
                                                                                
  Architecture must remain:                                                     
                                                                                
  UI → server action/application service → domain → repository → Prisma         
                                                                                
  Email delivery must remain an infrastructure concern.                         
                                                                                
  ## 3. V1 notification events                                                  
                                                                                
  Implement the events already defined by the project documentation:            
                                                                                
  ### Customer emails                                                           
                                                                                
  * email verification                                                          
  * password reset                                                              
  * order confirmation                                                          
  * quote sent                                                                  
  * quote expiring                                                              
                                                                                
  Also add customer-facing transactional emails for important existing          
  lifecycle events **only if they are explicitly supported by the current       
  BUSINESS-RULES/ROADMAP**.                                                     
                                                                                
  Do not invent additional event types.                                         
                                                                                
  ### Staff/admin notifications                                                 
                                                                                
  Implement the existing operational notifications:                             
                                                                                
  * `request.submitted`                                                         
  * `quote.expiring`                                                            
  * `order.placed`                                                              
                                                                                
  Use the existing notification requirements from the docs rather than          
  inventing new recipients or workflows.                                        
                                                                                
  ## 4. Important architecture rules                                            
                                                                                
  Do not put email sending directly into:                                       
                                                                                
  * React components                                                            
  * route handlers                                                              
  * repositories                                                                
  * Prisma callbacks                                                            
  * database triggers                                                           
                                                                                
  Create a small application/infrastructure seam such as:                       
                                                                                
  * notification event creation                                                 
  * notification dispatcher                                                     
  * email renderer/template layer                                               
  * email provider adapter                                                      
                                                                                
  Use the project's existing architecture and naming conventions if equivalent  
  abstractions already exist.                                                   
                                                                                
  The domain/application layer should describe **what happened**.               
                                                                                
  Infrastructure decides **how the email is delivered**.                        
                                                                                
  ## 5. Reliability                                                             
                                                                                
  Email delivery must not break the underlying business transaction.            
                                                                                
  For example:                                                                  
                                                                                
  * placing an order must succeed even if the email provider is temporarily     
  unavailable                                                                   
  * sending a quote must not roll back because email delivery failed            
  * recording a payment must not depend on email availability                   
                                                                                
  Use an appropriate durable/outbox-style mechanism if the current architecture 
  supports it.                                                                  
                                                                                
  If a database-backed notification/outbox is required, design it carefully and 
  document the decision.                                                        
                                                                                
  Do not introduce a heavyweight queue system unless genuinely necessary for    
  V1.                                                                           
                                                                                
  At minimum ensure:                                                            
                                                                                
  * no duplicate sends from normal retries                                      
  * safe retry behavior                                                         
  * idempotent event handling                                                   
  * failed delivery can be retried                                              
  * sensitive data is not stored unnecessarily                                  
                                                                                
  ## 6. Email content                                                           
                                                                                
  Emails must use historical/business snapshots where appropriate.              
                                                                                
  Never reconstruct historical order/quote information from mutable catalogue   
  data.                                                                         
                                                                                
  For example, an order confirmation should use the same historical information 
  the order stores:                                                             
                                                                                
  * order number                                                                
  * purchased item names/variants                                               
  * quantities                                                                  
  * totals                                                                      
  * shipping information                                                        
  * payment/deposit position where appropriate                                  
                                                                                
  Quote emails should use the immutable quote/version data.                     
                                                                                
  Do not expose:                                                                
                                                                                
  * internal notes                                                              
  * staff-only information                                                      
  * storage keys                                                                
  * authentication/session secrets                                              
  * private administrative data                                                 
                                                                                
  ## 7. Quote expiration                                                        
                                                                                
  Implement the existing quote-expiring notification requirement.               
                                                                                
  Do not invent a new quote validity period.                                    
                                                                                
  Use the existing:                                                             
                                                                                
  * quote validity rules                                                        
  * `validUntil`                                                                
  * effective-status/lazy-expiration behavior                                   
                                                                                
  Avoid sending repeated expiration emails for the same quote.                  
                                                                                
  The existing quote state machine remains the source of truth.                 
                                                                                
  ## 8. Request/order admin notifications                                       
                                                                                
  Implement the existing staff notifications without leaking customer-sensitive 
  information.                                                                  
                                                                                
  ### `request.submitted`                                                       
                                                                                
  Notify the configured workshop/admin recipient when a new custom request is   
  submitted.                                                                    
                                                                                
  ### `quote.expiring`                                                          
                                                                                
  Notify the appropriate staff recipient according to the existing project      
  configuration.                                                                
                                                                                
  ### `order.placed`                                                            
                                                                                
  Notify the workshop/admin recipient when an order is successfully placed.     
                                                                                
  Use configuration/settings already present in the project where applicable.   
                                                                                
  Do not hard-code personal email addresses.                                    
                                                                                
  ## 9. Email provider                                                          
                                                                                
  Inspect the existing email seam first.                                        
                                                                                
  If no provider has been selected yet, create a provider-neutral adapter and a 
  development/test implementation.                                              
                                                                                
  Do NOT fabricate production credentials.                                      
                                                                                
  Do NOT integrate an arbitrary provider merely for demonstration.              
                                                                                
  Environment variables must be validated through the existing env              
  configuration approach.                                                       
                                                                                
  Tests must be able to run without sending real emails.                        
                                                                                
  ## 10. Templates                                                              
                                                                                
  Create reusable responsive HTML email templates with plain-text fallbacks.    
                                                                                
  Keep the visual language consistent with the existing STITCH design system,   
  but remember that email clients have limited CSS support.                     
                                                                                
  Templates should include:                                                     
                                                                                
  * brand identity                                                              
  * clear hierarchy                                                             
  * important action CTA where appropriate                                      
  * concise content                                                             
  * accessible contrast                                                         
  * plain-text equivalent                                                       
                                                                                
  Do not overdesign them.                                                       
                                                                                
  ## 11. Security/privacy                                                       
                                                                                
  Test:                                                                         
                                                                                
  * no auth tokens in email bodies                                              
  * no password/reset secret leakage beyond the intended reset mechanism        
  * no internal notes                                                           
  * no private storage URLs exposed permanently                                 
  * no cross-customer information                                               
  * no forged event recipient                                                   
  * notification authorization derives from trusted server-side data            
  * customer email comes from the authoritative order/request/quote owner data  
  * staff recipients cannot be changed by client payloads                       
                                                                                
  Do not trust client-supplied email addresses for notification routing.        
                                                                                
  ## 12. Testing                                                                
                                                                                
  Add strong tests for:                                                         
                                                                                
  ### Unit                                                                      
                                                                                
  * event creation                                                              
  * template rendering                                                          
  * recipient resolution                                                        
  * idempotency keys                                                            
  * quote-expiration logic                                                      
  * failure/retry behavior                                                      
  * plain-text generation                                                       
                                                                                
  ### Integration                                                               
                                                                                
  * order placement creates the expected notification                           
  * quote sending creates the expected notification                             
  * request submission creates the expected notification                        
  * quote expiration notification is not duplicated                             
  * email failure does not roll back business transactions                      
  * retry succeeds                                                              
  * customer/staff recipients are server-derived                                
  * historical snapshots are used                                               
                                                                                
  ### E2E                                                                       
                                                                                
  Cover the most important customer-visible flows:                              
                                                                                
  1. custom request → appropriate notification                                  
  2. quote sent → customer receives/records notification                        
  3. order placed → confirmation notification                                   
  4. quote approaching expiration → expiration notification                     
                                                                                
  Use a fake/test email transport and assert on captured messages.              
                                                                                
  Do not make E2E dependent on a real external email provider.                  
                                                                                
  Run the full existing regression suite.                                       
                                                                                
  ## 13. Operational concerns                                                   
                                                                                
  Provide a minimal mechanism for:                                              
                                                                                
  * pending notification processing                                             
  * retrying failed notifications                                               
  * preventing duplicate sends                                                  
                                                                                
  If a scheduler/cron is needed, implement only the smallest V1-compatible seam 
  and document how it will be invoked.                                          
                                                                                
  Do not build a general job-processing platform.                               
                                                                                
  Do not add analytics, marketing email, abandoned-cart email, newsletters,     
  SMS, WhatsApp, push notifications, or customer preferences in this phase.     
                                                                                
  ## 14. Documentation                                                          
                                                                                
  Update only the relevant documentation:                                       
                                                                                
  * `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`                                                      
  * `docs/DATABASE.md` if schema changes                                        
  * `docs/SECURITY.md`                                                          
  * `docs/ROADMAP.md`                                                           
  * `tasks/CURRENT.md`                                                          
  * environment documentation if new variables are introduced                   
  * add an ADR only if a genuinely new architectural decision is introduced     
                                                                                
  Do not rewrite unrelated docs.                                                
                                                                                
  ## 15. Explicit exclusions                                                    
                                                                                
  Do NOT implement:                                                             
                                                                                
  * Phase 13 features                                                           
  * analytics                                                                   
  * marketing emails                                                            
  * newsletters                                                                 
  * abandoned-cart emails                                                       
  * SMS

  * admin catalogue management                                                  
  * admin dark mode                                                             
  * unrelated V2 features                                                       
                                                                                
  ## 16. Validation                                                             
                                                                                
  Before declaring completion:                                                  
                                                                                
  * typecheck                                                                   
  * lint                                                                        
  * unit tests                                                                  
  * integration tests                                                           
  * E2E tests

  Do not weaken existing tests to make the phase pass.

      629  Templates are typed by event, and every template declares its 
           audience so a customer template can
      630  never be dispatched to a staff recipient or the reverse.
      631  
      632 +### As built (Phase 8A)                                       
      633 +                                                              
      634 +The eight events above ship, split across four modules and one
          + route:                                                       
      635 +                                                              
      636 +| Piece                                    | What it holds    
          +                                                              
          +                                                              
          +                     |                                        
      637 +| ---------------------------------------- | -----------------
          +--------------------------------------------------------------
          +--------------------------------------------------------------
          +-------------------- |                                        
      638 +| `domain/notification`                    | Pure. The templat
          +e list, `TEMPLATE_AUDIENCE`, `dedupeKeyFor`, the retry curve, 
          +the expiry-warning window, recipient parsing. No I/O.         
          +                      |                                       
      639 +| `lib/email`                              | Rendering and tra
          +nsport. Tables and inline styles, a plain-text alternative for
          + every message, and one `EmailTransport` interface with three 
          +implementations.      |                                       
      640 +| `server/repositories/notification-*`     | The only module t
          +hat touches the `notifications` table. Re-exports `Notificatio
          +nTx` and `NotificationPayload` so a service can join a transac
          +tion without Prisma.  |                                       
      641 +| `server/services/notification-service`   | Enqueuing (in the
          + caller's transaction) and dispatching (out of band). Holds no
          + business rules — it asks the domain who a template is for and
          + when to retry.       |                                       
      642 +| `POST /api/jobs/notifications`           | The scheduler's o
          +ne door. Bearer `JOBS_SECRET`, compared in constant time; 503 
          +when unconfigured. Every five minutes is enough.              
          +                      |                                       
      643 +                                                              
      644 +Eight templates rather than the table's seven names: `quote.ex
          +piring` is one row above per                                  
      645 +audience, and they are two templates here — `quote.expiring` a
          +nd `quote.expiring.staff` — because                           
      646 +a flag is how the customer's copy eventually reaches the works
          +hop's inbox.                                                  
      647 +                                                              
      648 +**Idempotency** is a unique `dedupe_key`, `<template>:<busines
          +s id>` (plus the address for a staff                          
      649 +row, so two recipients are two deliveries). The writer swallow
          +s the unique violation, so enqueuing                          
      650 +the same event twice is one row rather than two e-mails. A swe
          +ep that runs every five minutes                               
      651 +across a two-day warning window therefore sends one warning.  
      652 +                                                              
      653 +**Claiming** is a per-row conditional update — `WHERE id = ? A
          +ND status = 'PENDING' AND                                     
      654 +scheduled_for <= now`, pushing `scheduled_for` forward by a fi
          +ve-minute lease — the same                                    
      655 +compare-and-swap the order and quote transitions use. Two disp
          +atchers running at once each send                             
      656 +every row at most once; a dispatcher that dies mid-batch relea
          +ses its rows when the lease lapses.                           
      657 +                                                              
      658 +**Retry** is four fixed backoffs (1, 5, 15, 60 minutes) and fi
          +ve attempts, then `FAILED` where a                            
      659 +person can find it and requeue it. An unrenderable row — unkno
          +wn template, payload that does not                            
      660 +fit it — fails immediately without retry, because it will not 
          +become well-formed on its own.                                
      661 +                                                              
      662 +**The two credential e-mails bypass the outbox** and are sent 
          +inline. See ADR-022.                                          
      663 +                                                              
      664 +**Recipients are never an argument a browser can supply.** A c
          +ustomer e-mail goes to the row's own                          
      665 +address (the order's stored `contactEmail`, the quote owner's 
          +account address); a staff e-mail goes                         
      666 +to the `notifications.adminRecipients` setting, with `ADMIN_NO
          +TIFICATION_EMAIL` as a bootstrap for                          
      667 +a deployment where nobody has opened the admin yet. With neith
          +er configured a `SKIPPED` row is                              
      668 +written, so an undelivered event is visible rather than silent
          +.                                                             
      669 +                                                              
      670 +The sweep also runs Phase 10's two lazy expiry sweeps first, s
          +o a warning is never sent about a                             
      671 +quote that has already lapsed. Warnings queued during a sweep 
          +go out on the next one: dispatch uses                         
      672 +the instant the sweep began.                                  
      673 +                                                              
      674 +Not built, and deliberately: the `acceptanceValidUntil` warnin
          +g in the table above (V1 warns before                         
      675 +`validUntil` only — the acceptance window is short and is enfo
          +rced lazily on every read), in-app                            
      676 +notification records, and the WhatsApp link generator.
